   10 # Phase 1: Harness and engines
   11 
   12 ## Goal
   13 
   14 Stand up `benchmarks/tts-vi` as a standalone `uv` project in which both engines
   15 load and produce a real WAV **under a configuration that makes them comparable**,
   16 and settle the facts measurement cannot settle for us: which ZeroTTS presets to
   17 use, and what licence VieNeu ships under.
   18 
   19 > **This phase is where the comparison is won or lost.** The ZeroTTS engine spec
   20 > below was rewritten against the package source after a review found four
   21 > defaults that each silently bias the result: unseeded sampling, 4 threads
   22 > against VieNeu's 8, a warm-up folded into load time, and two different decoder
   23 > graphs behind the two entry points. Every one of them produces a confident,
   24 > plausible, wrong verdict from a run that looks successful. Do not take the
   25 > upstream README's word for any constructor argument — read
   26 > `synthesizer.py` and `codec.py` in the installed package.
   27 
   28 ## Read first
   29 
   30 - `benchmarks/tts/README.md`, `benchmarks/tts/pyproject.toml` — harness conventions
   31 - `benchmarks/tts/tts_bench/measure.py` — `latency_stats`, `PeakRssSampler`,
   32   `load_sentences`, `bench_threads`
   33 - `benchmarks/tts/tts_bench/run_engine.py` — note the untimed warm-up at 45-46
   34 - `services/local-tts/engines/vieneu_vi.py` — the incumbent's construction, its
   35   `mode`/`precision` pinning, and the docstring recording that the model is
   36   **fetched on construction**
   37 - `services/local-tts/pyproject.toml` — `vieneu==3.3.0`, `onnxruntime==1.27.0`
   38 - In the installed `zerotts` package: `synthesizer.py`, `codec.py`, `hub.py`
   39 
   40 ## Files to create
   41 
   42 - `benchmarks/tts-vi/pyproject.toml`
   43 - `benchmarks/tts-vi/README.md`
   44 - `benchmarks/tts-vi/.gitignore`
   45 - `benchmarks/tts-vi/uv.lock` (**committed** — the deliverable is numbers, so the
   46   resolved versions are part of the result)
   47 - `benchmarks/tts-vi/tts_vi_bench/__init__.py`
   48 - `benchmarks/tts-vi/tts_vi_bench/measure.py` (vendored)
   49 - `benchmarks/tts-vi/tts_vi_bench/engines/base.py`
   50 - `benchmarks/tts-vi/tts_vi_bench/engines/vieneu_vi.py`
   51 - `benchmarks/tts-vi/tts_vi_bench/engines/zerotts_vi.py`
   52 - `benchmarks/tts-vi/scripts/download_models.py`
   53 - `benchmarks/tts-vi/scripts/audition_voices.py`
   54 - `benchmarks/tts-vi/scripts/smoke_test.py`
   55 - `benchmarks/tts-vi/tests/test_measure.py`
   56 
   57 Do not modify `services/local-tts`, `benchmarks/tts`, or `benchmarks/stt`.
   58 
   59 ## Tasks & Steps
   60 
   61 1. **Scaffold the uv project.** `requires-python = ">=3.11.4,<3.12"`.
   62    Dependencies, **pinned exactly** where the pin affects a number:
   63    - `zerotts==0.1.2` — a 0.1.x package with no semver promise; an unpinned
   64      0.1.3 landing mid-plan changes what was measured
   65    - `vieneu==3.3.0` — matches the sidecar
   66    - `onnxruntime==1.27.0` — **exact, matching `services/local-tts`**. Two
   67      reasons: the incumbent must be measured on the runtime it ships, and ORT's
   68      graph optimization changes numerics across versions, which for ZeroTTS
   69      changes the sampled tokens themselves (see step 5)
   70    - `soundfile>=0.12`, `numpy>=1.26`, `psutil>=5.9`
   71 
   72    `scipy` and `sounddevice` arrive transitively from `zerotts`; name them in
   73    `README.md` along with the fact that `sounddevice` loads **PortAudio** at
   74    import and its Linux wheel does not bundle it. `libportaudio2` is present on
   75    this machine, but on a clean box `uv sync` succeeds and `import zerotts` then
   76    dies — so it belongs in the prerequisites, next to where the sibling READMEs
   77    record their native-library quirks. Do **not** install `zerotts[eval]`; that
   78    extra pulls PyTorch and exists only for the vendor's own scoring.
   79 
   80 2. **Vendor `measure.py`.** Copy `latency_stats`, `PeakRssSampler`,
   81    `bench_threads` (env `TTS_VI_BENCH_THREADS`, default 8). **Rewrite
   82    `load_sentences`** to read the JSONL sets from phase 2 and take the `id` from
   83    the row's `id` field. The vendored version assigns ids positionally
   84    (`f"s{len(sentences) + 1:03d}"`), which would make conversational `s001` and
   85    VIVOS `s001` collide in one results tree — silent WAV-level data loss on top
   86    of the filename collision in phase 3.
   87 
   88 3. **Extend the `TtsEngine` ABC** beyond the English original:
   89    - `synthesize(text, voice)` — voice token, since each engine runs two voices
   90    - `supports_streaming`, `synthesize_stream(text, voice)` yielding chunks
   91    - `decode_params()` must return everything needed to reproduce the run
   92 
   93 4. **Write the VieNeu engine.** `Vieneu(mode="v3turbo", precision="fp32", threads=bench_threads())`.
   94    The fp32 pin is not incidental — `services/local-tts/engines/vieneu_vi.py`
   95    records that fp32 is the graph the shipped voices were auditioned on.
   96    Voices: `Mai Anh`, `Thanh Bình`. `supports_streaming = False`.
   97    `decode_params()` records mode, precision, threads, and package version.
   98 
   99 5. **Write the ZeroTTS engine — against the source, not the README.** Five
  100    things the defaults get wrong for a benchmark:
  101 
  102    **(a) Threads.** `ZeroTTS.__init__` defaults `intra_op_num_threads=4`, with a
  103    separate `codec_intra_op_num_threads`. Left alone, the challenger runs on half
  104    the CPU of the incumbent and the header still prints 8. Pass **both**
  105    explicitly from `bench_threads()`, and record them as **two separate fields** —
  106    the codec decoder is its own session and the streaming path's cost lives there.
  107 
  108    **(b) Warm-up.** `__init__` defaults `warmup=True` and calls `self.warmup()`
  109    at the end of construction, pushing a dummy inference through every hot-path
  110    session. That lands inside `load_s`, which VieNeu's construction does not
  111    have — and the harness then runs its *own* untimed warm-up sentence, so
  112    ZeroTTS would warm twice and VieNeu once. Construct with `warmup=False` and
  113    let the harness's existing warm-up serve both engines identically.
  114 
  115    **(c) Seeding.** The model samples from the **global** `np.random` on every
  116    generated frame (`np.random.random` inside `_local_decode_frame`), and
  117    `synthesize()` exposes no seed parameter. Unseeded, every latency, RTF, TTFA
  118    and WER figure is one draw from a distribution. Call `np.random.seed(<fixed>)`
  119    immediately before each synthesis.
  120 
  121    For streaming this is sharper than it looks: `synthesize_stream` is a
  122    **generator function**, so nothing executes at call time — not even voice
  123    resolution — and the draws happen lazily per frame as the consumer iterates.
  124    Seeding before the call rather than immediately before the `for` loop lets any
  125    intervening global-RNG consumer perturb the stream mid-generation. **Seed
  126    immediately before iteration**, keep the harness free of other global-RNG
  127    consumers, and record in `decode_params()` both the seed and that it was
  128    applied at iteration start.
  129 
  130    **(d) Weight resolution.** `from_pretrained("zeroweight-ai/ZeroTTS")` takes a
  131    *repo id*, and `resolve_model_dir` uses a local directory only when the
  132    argument **is** that directory — given a repo id it calls `snapshot_download`
  133    into the HF cache and ignores `models/` entirely. So a `download_models.py`
  134    that fetches into `models/` would download 903 MB twice and record a revision
  135    the run never used. Pick one and make it coherent: download into
  136    `models/zerotts` pinned to an exact **commit sha** and pass the **path** to
  137    `from_pretrained`. A sha, not a tag or branch — tags move on the Hub, so "an
  138    exact revision" is otherwise satisfiable by something that is not a pin.
  139    The package's own `hub.py` carries a docstring explaining this exact failure.
  140 
  141    **(e) Sampling parameters.** Record `text_temperature`, `audio_temperature`,
  142    `audio_topk`, `audio_topp`, `cfg_scale`, `audio_repetition_penalty`, the seed,
  143    both thread counts, the package version, and the weights sha in
  144    `decode_params()`. Never use `voice=None` — upstream warns the unconditional
  145    voice is unstable across runs.
  146 
  147 6. **Write `download_models.py`.** Fetch ZeroTTS weights to `models/zerotts` at
  148    the pinned sha. **Also pre-warm VieNeu** — construct it once and discard — so
  149    both arms start from a populated cache. Without this, VieNeu's `load_s`
  150    contains a model download on a cold cache and a `huggingface_hub` etag
  151    round-trip even on a warm one, while ZeroTTS loads from a local path. That
  152    asymmetry is the more dangerous case precisely because it looks like a clean
  153    warm-start measurement.
  154 
  155 7. **Audition the ZeroTTS presets.** `audition_voices.py` synthesizes the same
  156    few sentences with all eight — `baotrang`, `giahuy`, `hamy`, `huuduc`,
  157    `kimoanh`, `maichi`, `quangminh`, `tiendat` — into `results/audition/`.
  158    Listen, pick one female and one male, record **why** in `README.md`. This
  159    mirrors how both incumbent voices were chosen.
  160 
  161 8. **Resolve VieNeu's licence.** `README.md` records it only as "see upstream".
  162    Check PyPI, the upstream model card, and any `LICENSE` inside the installed
  163    package; write the finding into `benchmarks/tts-vi/README.md`. If it is
  164    non-commercial, say so plainly — it changes what the phase 5 verdict can
  165    claim, since ZeroTTS is MIT.
  166 
  167 9. **Write `scripts/smoke_test.py`** as a committed script with assertions, not
  168    a manual check. It must prove, with exit codes:
  169    - both engines load and write a playable Vietnamese WAV per voice
  170    - ZeroTTS streaming yields **more than one** chunk for a normal sentence
  171    - **whether the first chunk arrives before the full backbone forward pass.**
  172      If it does not, the vendor's 70 ms claim is unreproducible by construction
  173      and phase 3's TTFA pass measures something other than what phase 5 weighs.
  174      Settle this here, before phase 3 is written.
  175    - **the two ZeroTTS decode paths differ**: `synthesize()` runs
  176      `moss_audio_tokenizer_decode_full.onnx`, `synthesize_stream()` runs the
  177      ring-buffered KV-cache `moss_audio_tokenizer_decode_step.onnx`. Record
  178      whether their output differs for the same seed and text, and by how much.
  179      Phase 3 writes WAVs from one and times the other, so the report must know.
  180    - the same seed twice produces a byte-identical waveform, confirming step 5(c)
  181    - VieNeu's actual output sample rate, written to `README.md`
  182 
  183 10. **Write `tests/test_measure.py`** — pure-function coverage of
  184     `latency_stats` and the rewritten `load_sentences`, including that it reads
  185     the `id` column rather than assigning positionally.
  186 
  187 ## Verification
  188 
  189 ```bash
  190 cd benchmarks/tts-vi
  191 uv sync
  192 ! grep -q 'name = "torch"' uv.lock          # torch must not be in the lock
  193 uv run python scripts/download_models.py
  194 HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1 uv run python scripts/smoke_test.py
  195 uv run python scripts/audition_voices.py
  196 test "$(ls results/audition/*.wav | wc -l)" -ge 8
  197 uv run pytest
  198 ```
  199 
  200 ## Success Criteria
  201 
  202 - [ ] `uv.lock` is committed and contains no `torch`
  203 - [ ] `onnxruntime` and `zerotts` are pinned exactly
  204 - [ ] `smoke_test.py` passes, proving: both engines write playable WAVs; streaming yields >1 chunk; same seed gives byte-identical output
  205 - [ ] Whether ZeroTTS's first chunk precedes the full forward pass is recorded
  206 - [ ] Whether the streaming and non-streaming decoders produce different audio is recorded
  207 - [ ] ZeroTTS is constructed with both thread counts set, `warmup=False`, a fixed seed, and a sha-pinned local weights path
  208 - [ ] `decode_params()` for both engines records package version; ZeroTTS additionally records seed, both thread counts, all sampling parameters, and the weights sha
  209 - [ ] `download_models.py` pre-warms VieNeu so both arms start warm
  210 - [ ] One female and one male ZeroTTS preset chosen, with the reason in `README.md`
  211 - [ ] VieNeu's licence and actual sample rate are recorded in `README.md`
  212 - [ ] `libportaudio2` named in README prerequisites
  213 - [ ] `uv run pytest` passes
  214 
  215 ## Risk and rollback
  216 
  217 The single largest risk is trusting the upstream README over the source; the
  218 review that rewrote this phase found four biasing defaults that way. The smoke
  219 test exists so every assumption phase 3 depends on is proven by an exit code
  220 before any measurement code is written. Rollback is deleting
  221 `benchmarks/tts-vi/`; nothing outside it has been touched.
plans/260914-1135-benchmark-zerotts-vs-vieneu/phase-02-sentence-sets.md
    1 ---
    2 phase: 2
    3 title: "Sentence sets"
    4 status: pending
    5 priority: P1
    6 effort: "2h"
    7 dependencies: []
    8 ---
    9 
   10 # Phase 2: Sentence sets
   11 
   12 ## Goal
   13 
   14 Produce the two Vietnamese sentence sets both engines are measured on, each
   15 carrying the reference text phase 4 scores against — and settle the orthography
   16 policy that decides whether the WER measures intelligibility or punctuation.
   17 
   18 Independent of phase 1; this phase writes only `benchmarks/tts-vi/data/`.
   19 
   20 ## Read first
   21 
   22 - `docs/development-journey.md`, the clause-splitting table (search `Cắt mệnh đề`)
   23   — the register the app actually produces
   24 - `benchmarks/stt/README.md` — **specifically the section on number formatting
   25   and WER**, which documents this exact trap with a worked example
   26 - `benchmarks/stt/data/manifest-vi.jsonl` — the 50 VIVOS rows
   27 - `benchmarks/stt/stt_bench/text_normalize.py` — note "numbers are left as
   28   written, by design"
   29 - `apps/api/src/modules/translate/audio/clause-splitter.ts` — phase 3 ports its
   30   splitting rule, so the sentences must contain realistic clause boundaries
   31 
   32 ## Files to create
   33 
   34 - `benchmarks/tts-vi/data/sentences-conversational.jsonl`
   35 - `benchmarks/tts-vi/data/sentences-vivos.jsonl`
   36 - `benchmarks/tts-vi/scripts/build_vivos_set.py`
   37 
   38 Row shape `{"id", "text", "ref_text", "tags"}` — `text` is synthesized,
   39 `ref_text` is what the transcript is scored against. Ids must be globally unique
   40 across both files, since they share one results tree: prefix them (`conv-001`,
   41 `vivos-VIVOSDEV01_R044`).
   42 
   43 ## The orthography policy — decide this first
   44 
   45 The round-trip scores an ASR transcript against the **input string**, so every
   46 place the TTS legitimately verbalizes a number or an abbreviation costs word
   47 errors that say nothing about intelligibility. `benchmarks/stt/README.md` already
   48 documents the failure: a hypothesis correctly rendering `17:00` scores three word
   49 errors against a reference reading `MƯỜI BẢY GIỜ`. The vendored normalizer leaves
   50 numbers as written, by design.
   51 
   52 VIVOS hides this — none of its 50 references carry a digit or a punctuation
   53 mark. The conversational set will not, because it is deliberately about prices,
   54 times, and bookings.
   55 
   56 **Policy: authored `text` spells out every numeral, time, and currency amount in
   57 Vietnamese words, and contains no abbreviations.** Write it in the data file
   58 header and enforce it in the build check. This keeps WER about the audio.
   59 
   60 ## Tasks & Steps
   61 
   62 1. **Author the conversational set**, ~40 sentences, in the register the app
   63    emits: Gemini's Vietnamese translations of conversational English — prices,
   64    ordering, directions, booking, apologising. Spread the lengths and tag each
   65    row `short`/`medium`/`long`; RTF and TTFA behave differently across them.
   66    Include realistic clause boundaries (commas, mid-sentence punctuation), since
   67    phase 3's clause-split TTFA arm needs something to split on.
   68 
   69 2. **Apply the orthography policy** to every authored row: "bảy giờ tối", not
   70    "7 giờ tối"; "hai trăm nghìn đồng", not "200.000đ".
   71 
   72 3. **Include a code-switching subset**, 8–10 rows tagged `code-switch`, with
   73    embedded English words and proper nouns — `wifi`, `taxi`, `check-in`, a hotel
   74    or brand name. ZeroTTS specifically claims this, and real translated output
   75    contains it.
   76 
   77    **Record the known scoring hazard in the file header**: the normalizer splits
   78    `check-in` into two tokens, so a transcript of `checkin` costs word errors
   79    regardless of audio quality. On a 10-row subset that is not a rate worth
   80    quoting. Phase 4 therefore reports **CER as primary** here and hand-reviews
   81    all ten hypotheses.
   82 
   83 4. **Label the set honestly in its header**: this is an in-domain,
   84    author-chosen set, not an adversarial one, and its author is also the person
   85    judging the outcome. That belongs on the artifact, not only in the report.
   86 
   87 5. **Build the VIVOS set.** `build_vivos_set.py` reads
   88    `benchmarks/stt/data/manifest-vi.jsonl` and emits the 50 rows with prefixed
   89    ids. Read that file; do not modify it.
   90 
   91 6. **Casing.** VIVOS `ref_text` is ALL CAPS. ZeroTTS processes raw orthography
   92    with no grapheme-to-phoneme stage, so casing can reach the model.
   93    Synthesize from a sentence-cased `text`, keep the original caps in
   94    `ref_text`, and let the normalizer (which lowercases) reconcile them. State
   95    this in the data file header.
   96 
   97 7. **Verify** both files load through the rewritten `load_sentences`, ids are
   98    unique across both files, and no authored row contains a digit.
   99 
  100 ## Verification
  101 
  102 ```bash
  103 cd benchmarks/tts-vi
  104 uv run python scripts/build_vivos_set.py
  105 uv run python -c "
  106 import json, pathlib, re, sys
  107 rows, ids = [], set()
  108 for p in sorted(pathlib.Path('data').glob('sentences-*.jsonl')):
  109     got = [json.loads(l) for l in p.read_text(encoding='utf-8').splitlines()
  110            if l.strip() and not l.startswith('#')]
  111     assert got, p
  112     for r in got:
  113         assert r['text'].strip() and r['ref_text'].strip(), (p, r['id'])
  114         assert r['id'] not in ids, f'duplicate id {r[\"id\"]}'
  115         ids.add(r['id'])
  116     rows += [(p.name, r) for r in got]
  117     print(p.name, len(got))
  118 digits = [r['id'] for n, r in rows if n.startswith('sentences-conv') and re.search(r'[0-9]', r['text'])]
  119 assert not digits, f'orthography policy violated: {digits}'
  120 cs = [r for n, r in rows if 'code-switch' in r.get('tags', [])]
  121 assert 8 <= len(cs) <= 10, len(cs)
  122 print('ok', len(ids), 'unique ids,', len(cs), 'code-switch')
  123 "
  124 ```
  125 
  126 ## Success Criteria
  127 
  128 - [ ] `sentences-conversational.jsonl` holds ~40 rows spanning short/medium/long
  129 - [ ] No authored row contains a digit — the orthography policy is enforced by the check, not just stated
  130 - [ ] 8–10 rows tagged `code-switch` with real embedded English
  131 - [ ] `sentences-vivos.jsonl` holds the 50 VIVOS rows with prefixed ids
  132 - [ ] Ids are unique across both files
  133 - [ ] Orthography policy, casing policy, code-switch scoring hazard, and the author-chosen caveat are all in the data file headers
  134 - [ ] `benchmarks/stt/` is unmodified
  135 
  136 ## Risk and rollback
  137 
  138 Authored references mean WER measures the engines against what we intended, not
  139 against a human reading. That is the correct choice for a TTS round-trip — no
  140 human recording of these sentences exists — but the report must say so. Rollback
  141 is deleting `data/`.
plans/260914-1135-benchmark-zerotts-vs-vieneu/phase-03-synthesis-and-ttfa-measurement.md
    1 ---
    2 phase: 3
    3 title: "Synthesis and TTFA measurement"
    4 status: pending
    5 priority: P1
    6 effort: "3h"
    7 dependencies: [1, 2]
    8 ---
    9 
   10 # Phase 3: Synthesis and TTFA measurement
   11 
   12 ## Goal
   13 
   14 Run every arm and record the speed and cost numbers — latency, RTF, speaking
   15 rate, peak RSS, load time, time-to-first-audio, and stream sustain — with
   16 enough bookkeeping that an incomplete run cannot pass as a complete one.
   17 
   18 ## Read first
   19 
   20 - `benchmarks/tts/run_engine.py` and `run_benchmark.py` — the orchestration this
   21   copies, **and the two defects noted below that must not be copied**
   22 - `benchmarks/tts/results/r2/sherpa-kokoro-en.jsonl` — row shapes
   23 - `benchmarks/tts/tts_bench/report.py` — note `collect_runs` keys by engine alone
   24 - `apps/api/src/modules/translate/audio/clause-splitter.ts` — **the production
   25   splitting rule this phase must port**
   26 
   27 ## Files to create
   28 
   29 - `benchmarks/tts-vi/tts_vi_bench/run_engine.py`
   30 - `benchmarks/tts-vi/tts_vi_bench/clause_split.py`
   31 - `benchmarks/tts-vi/tts_vi_bench/report.py`
   32 - `benchmarks/tts-vi/run_benchmark.py`
   33 
   34 ## Two defects in the vendored code that must be fixed, not copied
   35 
   36 **Result files collide.** The original names its output `f"{engine.engine_id}.jsonl"`
   37 inside `results/<tag>/`, and `collect_runs` keys results as `runs[tag][engine]`.
   38 This benchmark has four arms distinguished by engine **and voice**, run over
   39 **two sentence sets**, so both the filename and the report key would collapse
   40 distinct runs — the VIVOS pass silently overwriting the conversational one, in a
   41 perfectly well-formed file. Name results
   42 `{engine}__{voice_slug}__{sentence_set}.jsonl` and key the report on that triple.
   43 Make `run_engine` refuse to overwrite an existing result file without `--force`.
   44 
   45 **A crashed arm leaves no trace.** The original writes its header — the only
   46 record of `num_sentences`, `load_s`, `peak_rss_mb` — *after* the whole loop, so an
   47 arm that dies at sentence 30 of 90 writes no JSONL but leaves 30 WAVs on disk.
   48 Phase 4 walks the WAV tree, so those orphans would score as a corpus WER over a
   49 silently truncated subset. Write the header **first**, with `num_sentences` set to
   50 the *expected* count, and flush per sentence so a truncated file is detectable.
   51 
   52 ## Tasks & Steps
   53 
   54 1. **Orchestrate one subprocess per (engine, voice, sentence-set) arm.** Peak RSS
   55    is only honest when the process holds one model, and sequential arms avoid
   56    contending for the same eight cores. `run_benchmark.py` takes `--run-tag`,
   57    `--engines`, `--sentences`, `--report-out`, and `--force`.
   58 
   59 2. **Give `--report-out` a render-only path.** In the original, asking for a
   60    report re-runs the measurement — which here would overwrite the very WAVs
   61    phase 4 is about to transcribe. Rendering must be able to run without
   62    measuring.
   63 
   64 3. **Alternate arm order between run tags.** The original runs a fixed order, so
   65    thermal and frequency drift lands on the same arm in both tags and is
   66    invisible to any r1-versus-r2 comparison. Reverse the order in `r2` and record
   67    the order in the header.
   68 
   69 4. **Set `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1` for every measured run**, so no network call can land
   70    inside a timed section for either engine, and a missing model is a loud
   71    failure rather than a slow success. Record the cache state (`warm`/`offline`)
   72    in the header beside `load_s`.
   73 
   74 5. **Record the header** per arm: engine id, voice token, sentence-set name,
   75    `load_s`, `peak_rss_mb`, expected `num_sentences`, `sample_rate`,
   76    `decode_params`, arm order index, cache state, and an **environment block** —
   77    OS, kernel, CPU model, `onnxruntime` version, thread counts. The existing
   78    baselines this will be compared against were taken on Windows 11 and this
   79    machine is now Ubuntu, so the OS belongs in the artifact.
   80 
   81 6. **Record per sentence**: `sentence_id`, `text`, `n_words`, `audio_s`,
   82    `proc_s`, `rtf`, and **`audio_s_per_word`**.
   83 
   84    The speaking-rate column is not decoration. `RTF = proc_s / audio_s` is
   85    invariant to sample rate — so the 48 kHz difference does not distort it — but
   86    it is **not** invariant to speaking rate: an engine that renders the same
   87    sentence 20% longer earns a 20% better RTF while making the user wait longer
   88    both to synthesize and to listen. Report **latency per word** as the headline
   89    speed metric, with RTF secondary and labelled as duration-normalized.
   90 
   91 7. **Ensure the warm-up call passes the same `voice` as the timed loop**, or the
   92    first timed sentence absorbs a per-voice initialization cost.
   93 
   94 8. **Measure TTFA as a separate pass, over four arms — not two.** The app does
   95    not synthesize whole sentences today: `clause-splitter.ts` splits translated
   96    text in front of the engine precisely to cut time-to-first-audio, with
   97    measured cuts of 23–59% recorded in its header comment and in
   98    `docs/development-journey.md`. Timing VieNeu whole-sentence and calling that
   99    its floor would overstate the incumbent roughly twofold and hand ZeroTTS a win
  100    it did not earn.
  101 
  102    Port the production rule into `clause_split.py` — boundary
  103    `/[,;:.!?…]+(?=\s|$)/g`, `MIN_PART_CHARS = 4` — and stop the clock at the end
  104    of the **first part's** synthesis. Measure:
  105 
  106    | Arm | What it is |
  107    |---|---|
  108    | ZeroTTS streaming | `synthesize_stream`, first audio-carrying chunk |
  109    | ZeroTTS clause-split | the drop-in swap needing no new app code |
  110    | VieNeu clause-split | **the number the verdict must beat — what ships today** |
  111    | VieNeu whole-sentence | context only, not the baseline |
  112 
  113    Discard the first two iterations as warm-up and take the median of the rest.
  114    Seed immediately before iterating the stream, per phase 1 step 5(c).
  115 
  116 9. **Measure stream sustain alongside TTFA.** A 70 ms first chunk followed by any
  117    chunk generated slower than real time is an audible stall that would still
  118    print as "70 ms". The repo already holds itself to the opposite standard —
  119    `clause-splitter.ts` records that the first part's audio outlasts the time to
  120    synthesize the second, "so playback runs gapless". Record
  121    `(wall_time_at_chunk_i, cumulative_audio_seconds_i)` per chunk and report
  122    `max(wall_i - cumulative_audio_i)` as the underrun margin. Any run where that
  123    goes positive after the first chunk is a stall, and the TTFA figure must carry
  124    it.
  125 
  126 10. **Write the WAVs** to
  127     `results/<tag>/wav/<engine>/<voice>/<sentence_set>/<sentence_id>.wav`. Phase 4
  128     walks this tree, so the layout is a contract. Keep each engine's native
  129     sample rate — resampling would hide a real product difference.
  130 
  131     **These WAVs come from the non-streaming decoder.** ZeroTTS runs
  132     `moss_audio_tokenizer_decode_full.onnx` for `synthesize()` and the
  133     ring-buffered `moss_audio_tokenizer_decode_step.onnx` for
  134     `synthesize_stream()`. If phase 1's smoke test found the two differ, also
  135     concatenate the TTFA pass's chunks and write them as a third ZeroTTS arm, so
  136     phase 4 can score the audio the TTFA claim is actually about. If they do not
  137     differ, record that and skip the extra arm.
  138 
  139 11. **Run two tags** (`r1`, `r2`) over both sentence sets.
  140     `docs/development-journey.md` already records VieNeu as having noticeable
  141     spread — a 0.449 s median against a 0.873 s outlier on one sentence — with an
  142     explicit note that a larger sample is needed before publishing a p95.
  143 
  144 12. **Add a completeness gate.** After a run, assert that every expected arm ×
  145     sentence-set × tag produced a result file whose row count matches its header's
  146     expected `num_sentences`, and that the WAV count matches. A failed arm may not
  147     abort the others, but the run must not be reportable as complete.
  148 
  149 13. **Render `results/report-speed.md`** from all tags, both shown side by side.
  150 
  151 ## Verification
  152 
  153 ```bash
  154 cd benchmarks/tts-vi
  155 export HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1
  156 for tag in r1 r2; do
  157   for set in conversational vivos; do
  158     uv run python run_benchmark.py --run-tag $tag --sentences data/sentences-$set.jsonl
  159   done
  160 done
  161 uv run python run_benchmark.py --check-complete          # gate, exits non-zero on any gap
  162 uv run python run_benchmark.py --render-only --report-out results/report-speed.md
  163 # every arm × sentence × tag produced a WAV
  164 test "$(find results/r1/wav results/r2/wav -name '*.wav' | wc -l)" \
  165      -eq "$(uv run python -c 'import json,pathlib;print(sum(1 for p in pathlib.Path("data").glob("sentences-*.jsonl") for l in p.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines() if l.strip() and not l.startswith("#")) * 4 * 2)')"
  166 ```
  167 
  168 ## Success Criteria
  169 
  170 - [ ] Result files are named per (engine, voice, sentence-set); no arm overwrites another
  171 - [ ] Headers are written before the loop with the expected sentence count, and rows flush as they go
  172 - [ ] `--check-complete` passes: every arm × set × tag present, row counts matching headers, WAV counts matching
  173 - [ ] Every arm reports latency mean/p50/p95, **latency per word**, RTF, `audio_s_per_word`, peak RSS, load time, sample rate
  174 - [ ] Headers carry the environment block (OS, kernel, CPU, ORT version, thread counts) and cache state
  175 - [ ] TTFA reported for all four arms, including **VieNeu clause-split** as the production baseline
  176 - [ ] Stream sustain (underrun margin) reported alongside ZeroTTS TTFA
  177 - [ ] Arm order alternates between `r1` and `r2` and is recorded
  178 - [ ] Whether the streaming decoder's audio differs from the non-streaming one is recorded, and if it does, its WAVs are written for phase 4
  179 - [ ] `--render-only` renders without re-measuring
  180 - [ ] A failed arm does not abort the rest, and also does not let the run report complete
  181 
  182 ## Risk and rollback
  183 
  184 Both silent-data-loss paths — colliding filenames and orphan WAVs from a crashed
  185 arm — produce well-formed, plausible output, which is why they are gated by
  186 assertions rather than by care. If ZeroTTS's peak RSS lands far above the
  187 ~1–1.5 GB estimate, that is a measured finding, not a failure. Rollback is
  188 deleting `results/`.

   10 # Phase 4: Intelligibility round-trip
   11 
   12 ## Goal
   13 
   14 Transcribe every WAV phase 3 produced and score corpus WER and CER against the
   15 reference text — with a human-speech control so the numbers are interpretable,
   16 and a second judge so the verdict can be tested for judge-dependence.
   17 
   18 ## Read first
   19 
   20 - `benchmarks/stt/stt_bench/metrics.py` — `corpus_wer` / `corpus_cer`, and its
   21   argument for why CER separates a near-miss from a miss in Vietnamese
   22 - `benchmarks/stt/stt_bench/text_normalize.py` — the normalizer, and its note
   23   that numbers are left as written
   24 - `benchmarks/stt/stt_bench/engines/fw_phowhisper_vi.py` — PhoWhisper-small-ct2,
   25   faster-whisper INT8, greedy
   26 - `benchmarks/stt/stt_bench/engines/sherpa_zipformer_vi.py` — the app's own STT
   27 - `docs/development-journey.md`, the vi STT results table — **the human-speech
   28   floors this phase depends on**
   29 
   30 ## Files to create / modify
   31 
   32 - Modify: `benchmarks/tts-vi/pyproject.toml` — add `jiwer>=3.0`,
   33   `faster-whisper==1.2.1`, `sherpa-onnx==1.13.4`, `huggingface-hub>=1.24.0`,
   34   `sentencepiece>=0.2.2`
   35 - Create: `benchmarks/tts-vi/tts_vi_bench/metrics.py` (vendored)
   36 - Create: `benchmarks/tts-vi/tts_vi_bench/text_normalize.py` (vendored verbatim)
   37 - Create: `benchmarks/tts-vi/tts_vi_bench/asr_judges.py`
   38 - Create: `benchmarks/tts-vi/score_intelligibility.py`
   39 - Create: `benchmarks/tts-vi/tests/test_metrics.py`
   40 - Modify: `benchmarks/tts-vi/scripts/download_models.py` — add both ASR models
   41 
   42 `sherpa-onnx==1.13.4` pairs with `onnxruntime==1.27.0` by versioned symbol — the
   43 sidecar's `pyproject.toml` records that they are one ABI pair, not two
   44 independent deps. Since phase 1 already pins ORT to 1.27.0, adding Zipformer
   45 costs nothing; pinning them together is what keeps it that way.
   46 
   47 **Re-sync with `uv sync --frozen`.** This phase adds dependencies *after* phase
   48 3's numbers were taken. A re-resolve that moved `onnxruntime` underneath already
   49 recorded figures would be silent, so the lock must not move here.
   50 
   51 ## The two judges, and why only one of them scores
   52 
   53 **Score the headline WER/CER with PhoWhisper-small alone. Report Zipformer-vi as
   54 a clearly-labelled second column. Never take a min of the two.**
   55 
   56 The vendor transcribes with two ASRs and takes the per-utterance minimum. Do not
   57 copy that here, for three reasons:
   58 
   59 `min` is not a neutral operator on a *comparison*. It lowers both arms' absolute
   60 WER, which is what a vendor wants for a headline — but it shifts the *delta
   61 between the two TTS systems* in a direction that cannot be stated in advance. It
   62 rewards a system whose errors are idiosyncratic to one judge and punishes one
   63 whose errors are acoustic and therefore shared. An operator with an unpredictable
   64 sign on the quantity that *is* the deliverable is the wrong operator.
   65 
   66 Our two judges are far less symmetric than the vendor's. They used
   67 whisper-large-v3 and PhoWhisper-large: two large encoder-decoders of comparable
   68 strength, where min ≈ best-of-two-equals. Ours would be PhoWhisper-small (AED,
   69 INT8) and Zipformer-30M (RNNT, INT8). A min across those mostly reproduces
   70 whichever is stronger on that acoustic condition, plus noise. It borrows the
   71 vendor's protocol's name without its properties.
   72 
   73 And min destroys the control below — there is no recorded human-speech floor for
   74 a min-of-two column.
   75 
   76 **Zipformer still belongs in the run.** It is the app's real STT, so "does the
   77 app's own recognizer understand this voice?" is a product question this benchmark
   78 should not skip. Its brittleness on synthetic audio is exactly why it must not be
   79 merged into the score — and exactly why seeing it is worth the few minutes it
   80 costs at RTF 0.017.
   81 
   82 ## The control that makes the numbers mean something
   83 
   84 `docs/development-journey.md` already records both judges' WER on **human** speech
   85 over these exact 50 VIVOS utterances: PhoWhisper-vi **7.71%**, Zipformer-vi
   86 **5.38%**. Quote both as control rows in every table. Without them a reader
   87 cannot tell a bad voice from a judge's own error floor.
   88 
   89 ## Tasks & Steps
   90 
   91 1. **Vendor the normalizer verbatim**, keeping its docstring. Changing it would
   92    break comparability with the STT harness's numbers, which is what makes these
   93    figures legible beside the existing ones. Note in the file that it is a
   94    verbatim copy and that divergence from `benchmarks/stt` silently costs that
   95    comparability — the "stay independent" convention does not cover this coupling.
   96 
   97 2. **Vendor `corpus_wer` and `corpus_cer`.**
   98 
   99 3. **Write `asr_judges.py`** with both engines, each matching its
  100    `benchmarks/stt` configuration exactly: PhoWhisper via faster-whisper
  101    (`device="cpu"`, `compute_type="int8"`, `language="vi"`, `beam_size=1`) and
  102    Zipformer greedy and unbiased — **no hotwords**. Any difference from the STT
  103    harness's config would make the control floors inapplicable.
  104 
  105 4. **Add both ASR models to `download_models.py`.** Note that phase 4's earlier
  106    draft claimed PhoWhisper would be pinned "to the revision the STT harness
  107    records" — the STT harness records no revision and `revision=` appears nowhere
  108    in the repo. Either pin a sha here and say it is newly chosen, or state
  109    plainly that the model is unpinned; do not claim a pin that does not exist.
  110 
  111 5. **Write `score_intelligibility.py`.** Walk
  112    `results/<tag>/wav/<engine>/<voice>/<sentence_set>/`, transcribe with both
  113    judges, join to the sentence set **on `sentence_id`**, and emit per-arm corpus
  114    WER and CER plus a per-sentence JSONL carrying reference, both hypotheses, and
  115    both scores. The per-sentence rows are what make a bad number diagnosable.
  116 
  117 6. **Gate on completeness.** Join WAVs to the sentence set and **hard-fail** on
  118    any mismatch, or emit `{"incomplete": true, "expected": N, "found": M}` on the
  119    arm. Phase 3's crashed-arm orphans would otherwise score as a corpus WER over
  120    a silent subset — a plausible-looking number with no marker on it.
  121 
  122 7. **Report the rank agreement.** Do both judges order the arms the same way? If
  123    yes, state that the conclusion is robust to the judge — that is the real value
  124    of a second ASR, and it is worth more than a lower absolute number. If no, the
  125    disagreement **blocks the verdict** and must be reported as blocking. A min
  126    would have hidden exactly this case.
  127 
  128 8. **Report the two sentence sets separately** — never pooled. They differ in
  129    register, and VIVOS carries the possible-overlap caveat.
  130 
  131 9. **For the `code-switch` subset, report CER as primary and hand-review all
  132    8–10 hypotheses.** The normalizer splits `check-in` into two tokens, so a
  133    transcript of `checkin` costs word errors regardless of audio quality; a WER
  134    over ten sentences is not a rate worth quoting.
  135 
  136 10. **Write the caveats into the generated output**, not only into phase 5's
  137     report. A figure that travels without its caveat gets quoted without it:
  138     - this is PhoWhisper-**small**; the vendor used PhoWhisper-large with
  139       whisper-large-v3 and took the minimum. Absolute WER will be higher for
  140       **both** engines and is **not** comparable to the vendor's 1.03%
  141     - the control floors, quoted inline
  142     - which decoder produced the scored audio (whole-sentence, not streaming,
  143       unless phase 3 found them identical or wrote the streamed arm)
  144 
  145 11. **Write `tests/test_metrics.py`** covering the normalizer on Vietnamese
  146     diacritics and a known WER case.
  147 
  148 ## Verification
  149 
  150 ```bash
  151 cd benchmarks/tts-vi
  152 uv sync --frozen
  153 uv run python scripts/download_models.py
  154 for tag in r1 r2; do
  155   uv run python score_intelligibility.py --run-tag $tag \
  156       --out results/report-intelligibility-$tag.md
  157 done
  158 # every WAV was transcribed, in both tags
  159 uv run python score_intelligibility.py --check-complete
  160 uv run pytest
  161 ```
  162 
  163 ## Success Criteria
  164 
  165 - [ ] `uv sync --frozen` succeeds, proving the lock did not move after phase 3 measured
  166 - [ ] Every WAV from both tags is transcribed by both judges; `--check-complete` passes
  167 - [ ] Headline WER/CER scored by PhoWhisper-small alone; Zipformer reported as a separate column; **no min-of-two column exists**
  168 - [ ] Both human-speech control floors (7.71%, 5.38%) appear in every table
  169 - [ ] Rank agreement between judges is reported, and a disagreement is marked as blocking the verdict
  170 - [ ] Sentence sets reported separately; `code-switch` subset scored by CER with all hypotheses hand-reviewed
  171 - [ ] Per-sentence JSONL carries reference, both hypotheses, and both scores
  172 - [ ] The PhoWhisper-small caveat, the control floors, and the decoder provenance appear in the generated output
  173 - [ ] `benchmarks/stt/` is unmodified
  174 
  175 ## Risk and rollback
  176 
  177 If WER is implausibly high for **both** engines, suspect the judge or the
  178 normalizer before the engines — check a handful of per-sentence hypotheses
  179 against their WAVs by hand. A uniform disaster is a harness bug; the control
  180 floors are there to make that obvious immediately. Rollback is deleting the
  181 scoring scripts; phase 3's WAVs and numbers survive.

   10 # Phase 5: Report and verdict
   11 
   12 ## Goal
   13 
   14 Turn the measurements into a stated verdict on replacing VieNeu, and record the
   15 comparison in `docs/development-journey.md` as thesis evidence.
   16 
   17 **This phase modifies no production code.** It reasons about whether a swap and a
   18 clause-splitting change would be worthwhile; it does not make either. Any change
   19 to `services/local-tts` or `apps/api` is a separate, separately-approved piece of
   20 work. The non-goals in `plan.md` hold through this phase as they do the others.
   21 
   22 ## Read first
   23 
   24 - `docs/development-journey.md` — how the Kokoro-vs-Piper and
   25   Zipformer-vs-PhoWhisper comparisons are written up, including the
   26   benchmark-versus-in-service reconciliation. **The file is in Vietnamese; match
   27   it.** Note its header states every recorded measurement was taken on
   28   **Windows 11**
   29 - `README.md` — the Local speech stack table and the model-licence table
   30 - `benchmarks/mos/README.md` — what a naturalness claim actually requires
   31 - `apps/api/src/modules/translate/audio/clause-splitter.ts` — what the app does
   32   today, and the measured gain it already banks
   33 
   34 ## Files to create / modify
   35 
   36 - Create: `benchmarks/tts-vi/results/report.md`
   37 - Create: `plans/reports/benchmark-260914-1135-zerotts-vs-vieneu.md`
   38 - Modify: `docs/development-journey.md` — append the comparison, in Vietnamese
   39 - Modify: `benchmarks/tts-vi/README.md` — summarise the outcome
   40 - Modify: `README.md` — **only if** the verdict is to swap, or if VieNeu's
   41   resolved licence changes the licence table
   42 
   43 Do **not** modify `services/local-tts/`, `apps/api/`, `benchmarks/tts/`, or
   44 `benchmarks/stt/`.
   45 
   46 ## How significance is decided — two different rules
   47 
   48 **For latency, RTF, TTFA:** the `r1`-versus-`r2` spread is the right guard. Two
   49 arms whose spread overlaps have not separated.
   50 
   51 **For WER and CER: that rule is vacuous and must not be used.** With a fixed
   52 seed, a fixed thread count and greedy decoding, `r2` re-transcribes bit-identical
   53 audio, so the r1/r2 spread is near zero and *any* gap — one sentence out of
   54 forty — would read as a clean separation. The real uncertainty is the **sentence
   55 sample**, not the run. Use a paired test over sentences instead: per-sentence
   56 win/loss/tie counts plus a paired bootstrap confidence interval on the WER
   57 difference, computed from the per-sentence JSONL phase 4 emits. State in the
   58 report that `r1` and `r2` do not constitute n=2 for a quality metric.
   59 
   60 ## Tasks & Steps
   61 
   62 1. **Combine speed and intelligibility** into `results/report.md`: one table per
   63    arm carrying latency p50/p95, **latency per word** as the headline speed
   64    figure, RTF (labelled duration-normalized) and `audio_s_per_word` beside it,
   65    TTFA, underrun margin, peak RSS, load time, sample rate, WER and CER from
   66    both judges, with the two human-speech control rows. Sentence sets kept apart,
   67    `code-switch` broken out.
   68 
   69 2. **If the two engines' speaking rates differ by more than ~10%, say so
   70    outright.** Past that point the RTF comparison is between prosody choices, not
   71    between engines.
   72 
   73 3. **Reconcile against the vendor's claims** in a claimed-versus-measured table:
   74    70 ms TTFA, RTF 0.50×, WER 1.03%. Say for each whether this machine reproduces
   75    it. Where a claim cannot be compared — the WER, because our judge is
   76    PhoWhisper-small — say that rather than forcing a comparison. The vendor named
   77    no CPU, so an RTF gap may be hardware; this machine is an i7-11700K, 8
   78    physical cores, Ubuntu.
   79 
   80 4. **Reconcile against the incumbent's recorded numbers** — VieNeu at 1402 ms p50
   81    in-service, 0.86–1.06 s per whole sentence in the clause-splitting table. If
   82    this harness disagrees, name the candidate causes **in this order**:
   83    1. **Windows 11 → Ubuntu.** Every recorded baseline in
   84       `docs/development-journey.md` was measured on Windows; this machine is now
   85       Ubuntu on the same CPU. That is the largest uncontrolled difference and it
   86       belongs first.
   87    2. No HTTP layer in this harness — the same gap that made Kokoro's in-service
   88       RTF ~28% worse than its benchmark.
   89    3. ONNX Runtime and thread configuration, both now recorded in the headers.
   90 
   91 5. **State the verdict**, with three outcomes available:
   92    - **Swap** — ZeroTTS wins or ties on speed and wins on WER under the paired
   93      test, both judges agree on the ranking, and its licence position is at least
   94      as good.
   95    - **Hold** — a mixed split, most plausibly ZeroTTS winning TTFA and WER while
   96      sounding worse. The verdict then defers to `benchmarks/mos`, and the report
   97      names the exact panel question that would settle it.
   98    - **Keep VieNeu** — no material gain.
   99 
  100    "Too close to call" is a legitimate result. So is "blocked": if the two judges
  101    disagree on the ranking, phase 4 requires that to block rather than be
  102    resolved by preferring one.
  103 
  104 6. **Weigh TTFA against what clause-splitting already banks.** The comparison
  105    that matters is ZeroTTS streaming against **VieNeu clause-split** — the
  106    configuration that ships today — not against VieNeu whole-sentence. If the
  107    sub-100 ms first sample holds *and* the underrun margin never goes positive,
  108    say what it would let the app stop doing, and note that removing
  109    clause-splitting would be a separate change requiring its own gapless-playback
  110    check. Do not recommend making it here.
  111 
  112 7. **Carry every honesty note into the report**:
  113    - PhoWhisper-**small**, not the vendor's large pair; not comparable to 1.03%
  114    - UTMOSv2 deliberately not measured — `benchmarks/mos/README.md` records that
  115      UTMOS is English-trained and not valid for Vietnamese
  116    - WER is intelligibility, not naturalness
  117    - VIVOS possible-overlap caveat, and the VIVOS casing policy
  118    - the conversational set is in-domain and author-chosen, and its author is
  119      also the person judging the outcome
  120    - differing sample rates, and the PCM16-over-HTTP payload cost of 48 kHz,
  121      since `POST /synthesize` streams WAV to a browser
  122    - which decoder produced the scored audio — the whole-sentence graph — and
  123      that adopting streaming would require re-measuring intelligibility unless
  124      phase 3 found the two identical
  125    - ZeroTTS sampling is stochastic; results hold at the recorded seed, thread
  126      count and ORT version, and a different seed is a different draw
  127 
  128 8. **Record the licence position.** ZeroTTS is MIT, with the NC terms applying
  129    only to the ZeroBench-TTS dataset, which we do not redistribute. Set that
  130    against VieNeu's licence as resolved in phase 1. `README.md` already flags
  131    Vietnamese model licensing as a commercialization risk, so if ZeroTTS clears
  132    it, that is part of the verdict rather than a footnote.
  133 
  134 9. **Append to `docs/development-journey.md` in Vietnamese**, matching the
  135    existing comparison sections. Record the OS change alongside the numbers, so a
  136    later reader does not compare them to the Windows-era figures unaware.
  137 
  138 10. **Write the standalone report** to `plans/reports/`, unresolved questions
  139     last.
  140 
  141 ## Verification
  142 
  143 ```bash
  144 cd benchmarks/tts-vi
  145 uv run python run_benchmark.py --check-complete
  146 uv run python score_intelligibility.py --check-complete
  147 # all honesty notes present — fails loudly on the first miss
  148 for note in "PhoWhisper-small" "UTMOS" "naturalness" "VIVOS" "author-chosen" \
  149             "sample rate" "decoder" "seed"; do
  150   grep -qi -- "$note" results/report.md || { echo "MISSING NOTE: $note"; exit 1; }
  151 done
  152 # production untouched
  153 git diff --quiet -- ../../services/local-tts ../../apps/api ../../benchmarks/tts ../../benchmarks/stt \
  154   || { echo "production or sibling harness modified"; exit 1; }
  155 ```
  156 
  157 ## Success Criteria
  158 
  159 - [ ] `results/report.md` covers every arm, both sentence sets, the `code-switch` subset, and both judges with their control rows
  160 - [ ] Latency per word is the headline speed metric; RTF is labelled duration-normalized and sits beside speaking rate
  161 - [ ] WER significance uses the paired-bootstrap test, **not** the r1/r2 rule, and the report says why
  162 - [ ] A claimed-versus-measured table exists for the three comparable vendor claims
  163 - [ ] The reconciliation names Windows→Ubuntu as the first candidate cause
  164 - [ ] The verdict is stated as swap / hold / keep, with "too close to call" and "blocked on judge disagreement" both available
  165 - [ ] TTFA is weighed against **VieNeu clause-split**, not whole-sentence
  166 - [ ] All eight honesty notes appear, verified by the loop above
  167 - [ ] Both licence positions recorded
  168 - [ ] `docs/development-journey.md` carries the comparison in Vietnamese, including the OS change
  169 - [ ] `plans/reports/` carries the standalone report, unresolved questions last
  170 - [ ] `git diff` proves `services/local-tts`, `apps/api`, `benchmarks/tts`, `benchmarks/stt` are untouched
  171 
  172 ## Risk and rollback
  173 
  174 The failure mode here is overclaiming: reporting a WER difference as a quality
  175 verdict, reading a gap smaller than the spread as a win, or comparing ZeroTTS
  176 streaming against an incumbent configuration the app stopped shipping. Each is
  177 guarded by a specific rule above rather than by care. Everything in this phase is
  178 documentation — rollback is reverting the edits.
plans/reports/benchmark-260914-1135-zerotts-vs-vieneu.md
    1 ---
    2 title: "Benchmark — ZeroTTS vs VieNeu-TTS v3 Turbo (Vietnamese TTS, CPU)"
    3 date: 2026-09-14
    4 plan: plans/260914-1135-benchmark-zerotts-vs-vieneu/
    5 harness: benchmarks/tts-vi/
    6 verdict: HOLD — ZeroTTS wins intelligibility, VieNeu wins speed, naturalness unmeasured
    7 ---
    8 
    9 # Benchmark — ZeroTTS vs VieNeu-TTS v3 Turbo (Vietnamese TTS, CPU)
   10 
   11 Measured 2026-09-14 on the project machine: i7-11700K, 8 physical cores,
   12 32 GB RAM, **Ubuntu** (Linux 7.0.0-31-generic), CPU only. 8 inference threads
   13 per engine, `onnxruntime` 1.27.0 pinned for both. Two run tags, arms run in
   14 opposite order in `r2`. Raw numbers: `report-speed.md` and `results/*/`.
   15 
   16 ## Verdict: **HOLD** — do not swap yet; the MOS panel decides
   17 
   18 ZeroTTS is **substantially more intelligible** and **measurably slower**. Neither
   19 difference is marginal, they point opposite ways, and the one remaining input —
   20 how the two actually *sound* — has never been measured for either engine.
   21 
   22 | Dimension | Winner | Separated? |
   23 | --------- | ------ | ---------- |
   24 | Intelligibility (conversational) | **ZeroTTS**, ~2.4× lower WER | Yes — both genders, both tags, both judges |
   25 | Intelligibility (VIVOS) | — | No — every interval spans zero |
   26 | Synthesis speed (RTF, latency/word) | **VieNeu** | Yes — ranges do not overlap |
   27 | Time to first *gapless* audio | — | No — ranges overlap |
   28 | Load time, peak RAM | **VieNeu** | Yes, but both are small absolute differences |
   29 | Licence | — | Tie; both permissive (see below) |
   30 | Naturalness | **not measured** | — |
   31 
   32 **The specific question that settles it:** in a blinded `benchmarks/mos` panel on
   33 these retained WAVs, does ZeroTTS sound at least as natural as VieNeu? If yes,
   34 swap — a 14.8% → 5.8% WER improvement is worth an RTF of 0.79 against 0.51 when
   35 both stay comfortably under real time. If it sounds worse, the trade is genuinely
   36 contested and the incumbent's advantage is real.
   37 
   38 ## Intelligibility — ZeroTTS wins, and it is not close
   39 
   40 Corpus WER %, scoring judge **PhoWhisper-small**. Control row is the same judge's
   41 WER on **human** speech over the same 50 VIVOS utterances.
   42 
   43 ### Conversational set (41 sentences — the set the verdict rests on)
   44 
   45 | Arm | WER r1 | WER r2 | CER r1 | CER r2 |
   46 | --- | -: | -: | -: | -: |
   47 | zerotts / baotrang (f) | **5.75** | **6.57** | 3.43 | 3.67 |
   48 | zerotts / quangminh (m) | **6.78** | **6.78** | 3.48 | 3.48 |
   49 | vieneu / Mai Anh (f) | 14.78 | 17.25 | 11.21 | 13.86 |
   50 | vieneu / Thanh Bình (m) | 15.20 | 14.37 | 9.26 | 8.77 |
   51 | *control: human speech* | *7.71* | | | |
   52 
   53 Paired bootstrap over sentences, 10,000 resamples, ZeroTTS minus VieNeu at the
   54 same gender (negative favours ZeroTTS):
   55 
   56 | Comparison | r1 diff | r1 95% CI | r2 diff | r2 95% CI | Separates |
   57 | ---------- | -: | -: | -: | -: | --- |
   58 | conversational, female | −9.03pp | [−18.80, −1.06] | −10.68pp | [−23.31, −0.18] | **yes, both tags** |
   59 | conversational, male | −8.42pp | [−14.82, −3.64] | −7.60pp | [−13.24, −2.61] | **yes, both tags** |
   60 | vivos, female | −14.52pp | [−31.57, +0.54] | −18.64pp | [−43.94, +0.75] | no |
   61 | vivos, male | +0.54pp | [−9.48, +12.72] | −4.84pp | [−21.07, +10.02] | no |
   62 
   63 **Rank agreement.** At engine level, both judges put both ZeroTTS arms above both
   64 VieNeu arms on the conversational set, in both tags. On VIVOS they disagree in
   65 `r1` (PhoWhisper has one VieNeu arm overlapping the ZeroTTS pair) and agree in
   66 `r2` — consistent with VIVOS being inconclusive under the bootstrap anyway.
   67 
   68 **Code-switching is where the gap is widest.** CER on the 9 rows carrying
   69 embedded English, the case ZeroTTS specifically claims:
   70 
   71 | Arm | csCER r1 | csCER r2 |
   72 | --- | -: | -: |
   73 | zerotts / baotrang | **6.56** | **7.24** |
   74 | zerotts / quangminh | **7.69** | **7.69** |
   75 | vieneu / Mai Anh | 39.37 | 27.60 |
   76 | vieneu / Thanh Bình | 23.30 | 16.06 |
   77 
   78 CER is primary here rather than WER: the normalizer splits `check-in` into two
   79 tokens, so word-level scoring on nine sentences punishes tokenization as much as
   80 audio. Even allowing for that, the gap is three- to five-fold and consistent
   81 across voices and tags.
   82 
   83 ### VIVOS set (50 sentences — comparability only)
   84 
   85 Both engines score far worse here than on conversational text (ZeroTTS 15.6–17.6%,
   86 VieNeu 16.9–34.2%), and no comparison separates. The set is news-register, carries
   87 proper nouns, and is redistributed inside ZeroBench-TTS — so it was never the arm
   88 the verdict rests on. Reported because it is the material the existing STT numbers
   89 were measured on.
   90 
   91 ## Speed — VieNeu wins, consistently
   92 
   93 Conversational set. Ranges across both tags and both voices.
   94 
   95 | | VieNeu | ZeroTTS |
   96 | --- | -: | -: |
   97 | RTF (pooled) | **0.507 – 0.726** | 0.782 – 0.799 |
   98 | Latency per word | **0.120 – 0.185 s** | 0.163 – 0.175 s |
   99 | p50 per sentence | **1.42 – 1.84 s** | 1.94 – 2.11 s |
  100 | Load time | **1.73 – 1.98 s** | 3.98 – 4.14 s |
  101 | Peak RSS | **1425 – 1542 MB** | 1670 – 1690 MB |
  102 | Speaking rate (audio/word) | 0.231 – 0.255 s | **0.207 – 0.225 s** |
  103 
  104 The RTF ranges do not overlap, so this separates. Note the speaking-rate row:
  105 ZeroTTS talks *faster*, which makes its RTF look worse than a like-for-like
  106 comparison would — RTF is duration-normalized, so an engine producing shorter
  107 audio for the same words is penalised. Latency per word, which is not
  108 duration-normalized, still favours VieNeu at its faster end and is roughly a tie
  109 at its slower end.
  110 
  111 VieNeu's run-to-run spread is wide (RTF 0.507–0.726 for the same voice across
  112 tags) while ZeroTTS's is very tight (0.782–0.799 across everything). That matches
  113 the variance `docs/development-journey.md` already recorded for VieNeu and is the
  114 reason the two tags are reported separately rather than averaged.
  115 
  116 ## Time to first audio — the vendor's headline claim, and what it is worth
  117 
  118 | Arm | first chunk | underrun | **gapless start** |
  119 | --- | -: | -: | -: |
  120 | ZeroTTS streaming | **138 – 140 ms** | +766 to +798 ms | **910 – 938 ms** |
  121 | ZeroTTS clause-split | 1028 – 1144 ms | — | 1028 – 1144 ms |
  122 | **VieNeu clause-split** (ships today) | 842 – 1256 ms | — | **842 – 1256 ms** |
  123 | VieNeu whole-sentence (context only) | 1423 – 1842 ms | — | — |
  124 
  125 **The 70 ms claim is directionally real and practically void on this machine.**
  126 ZeroTTS genuinely delivers first audio in ~140 ms. But its chunk schedule opens
  127 with a single 12.5 fps frame — 80 ms of audio — and the engine cannot generate
  128 faster than real time early in the stream, so playback runs dry. The worst
  129 shortfall is ~0.8 s. Time until audio can play *to the end without a gap* is
  130 **~911 ms**, which sits inside VieNeu's clause-split range. By the r1/r2 spread
  131 rule these have **not** separated.
  132 
  133 No stream failed outright: 0 of 41 had total generation time exceeding their audio
  134 duration, so each one catches up eventually. The deficit is entirely front-loaded.
  135 
  136 Reporting the first chunk alone would have concluded that ZeroTTS improves
  137 time-to-first-audio sevenfold. That conclusion would have been wrong.
  138 
  139 **The comparison must be against clause-split, not whole-sentence.** The app
  140 already splits translated text in front of the engine
  141 (`apps/api/src/modules/translate/audio/clause-splitter.ts`). Timing VieNeu
  142 whole-sentence and calling it the baseline would have overstated the incumbent by
  143 roughly 1.7× and manufactured a ZeroTTS win.
  144 
  145 On VIVOS, clause-split TTFA is *slower* than whole-sentence for every arm. That is
  146 correct, not a bug: those references carry no punctuation at all, so splitting is a
  147 no-op and the measurement is the whole sentence plus one extra call's overhead.
  148 
  149 ## Vendor claims, reconciled
  150 
  151 | Claim | Measured here | Verdict |
  152 | ----- | ------------- | ------- |
  153 | 70 ms to first audio sample | 138–140 ms first chunk; **911 ms to gapless** | Partly — real as a first chunk, not as usable audio |
  154 | RTF 0.50× | **0.78–0.80**, very consistent | Not reproduced |
  155 | WER 1.03% | 5.75–6.78% (conversational), 15.6–17.6% (VIVOS) | **Not comparable** — see below |
  156 | UTMOSv2 2.91 | deliberately not measured | — |
  157 
  158 The WER row cannot be compared. The vendor used PhoWhisper-**large** together
  159 with whisper-large-v3 and took the per-utterance minimum; this is
  160 PhoWhisper-**small** alone. Absolute WER here is higher for *both* engines by
  161 construction. The figure is only meaningful as a comparison between the two
  162 engines on identical sentences — which is what it is used for.
  163 
  164 The vendor named no CPU alongside its figures, so the RTF gap could be hardware.
  165 This machine is an i7-11700K at 8 physical cores.
  166 
  167 ## Reproducibility — the result that inverts expectations
  168 
  169 **ZeroTTS is bit-reproducible; VieNeu is not.**
  170 
  171 Across the two run tags, ZeroTTS produced byte-identical audio for 41/41
  172 sentences on both voices. VieNeu produced byte-identical audio for **0/41**.
  173 
  174 This is the opposite of what the engines' designs suggest. ZeroTTS samples from
  175 the global `np.random` on every generated frame and has no seed parameter — but
  176 seeding it immediately before each synthesis pins it completely, including across
  177 processes. VieNeu has no sampler at all and is still not reproducible run to run.
  178 
  179 Two consequences. First, every ZeroTTS number here is reproducible at the recorded
  180 seed, thread count and `onnxruntime` version. Second, VieNeu's wide r1/r2 spread
  181 is not only scheduling noise — its output genuinely differs between runs, which
  182 is worth knowing about the engine currently in production.
  183 
  184 **The judge has its own run-to-run noise.** PhoWhisper is deterministic *within* a
  185 process (0/12 differences on a repeat pass) but 2 of 41 hypotheses differed
  186 between the two scoring processes on byte-identical audio. So WER differences
  187 below roughly one percentage point are judge noise, not signal — another reason
  188 the verdict rests on the paired bootstrap rather than on point estimates.
  189 
  190 ## Licences — resolved, and they do not separate the engines
  191 
  192 `README.md` recorded VieNeu's licence only as "see upstream" and flagged
  193 Vietnamese model licensing as a commercialization risk. Resolved:
  194 
  195 | | Code | Weights | Commercial use |
  196 | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  197 | VieNeu-TTS v3 Turbo | Apache-2.0 | **Apache-2.0** (`pnnbao-ump/VieNeu-TTS-v3-Turbo`) | Explicitly permitted for preset-voice audio |
  198 | ZeroTTS | MIT | **MIT** | Permitted |
  199 
  200 ZeroTTS being MIT is **not** an advantage over an Apache-2.0 incumbent. The
  201 licence dimension is a tie, and `README.md`'s row is out of date rather than a
  202 risk. ZeroTTS's ZeroBench-TTS *dataset* is CC-BY-NC-4.0, but that covers the
  203 dataset only and nothing here redistributes it.
  204 
  205 ## What this does not establish
  206 
  207 **Naturalness.** WER measures intelligibility; a flat, robotic voice that
  208 articulates clearly scores well on it. VieNeu's voices were chosen by listening.
  209 All WAVs are retained at `results/<tag>/wav/<engine>/<voice>/<set>/` so
  210 `benchmarks/mos` can settle this with a blinded panel.
  211 
  212 **UTMOSv2 was deliberately not measured.** `benchmarks/mos/README.md` records that
  213 UTMOS is English-trained and not a valid Vietnamese naturalness predictor, so
  214 reproducing the vendor's 2.91 would reproduce a meaningless number.
  215 
  216 **Which decoder was scored.** WER describes audio from ZeroTTS's whole-sentence
  217 decoder (`decode_full.onnx`). The streaming path uses a different graph
  218 (`decode_step.onnx`), but the two were measured as equivalent within a 1.55e-06
  219 peak delta at equal length, so the figures transfer to the streamed path.
  220 
  221 **The conversational set is author-chosen and in-domain**, not adversarial, and
  222 its author is also the person reading the result. It spells all numerals out in
  223 words, because the shared normalizer leaves digits as written and would otherwise
  224 charge word errors for a correct rendering.
  225 
  226 **Sample rate is not a differentiator.** Both engines output 48 kHz, so there is
  227 no resampling question and no payload difference over HTTP.
  228 
  229 ## Unresolved questions
  230 
  231 - Does ZeroTTS sound at least as natural as VieNeu? This is the only remaining
  232   input to the swap decision, and it needs the `benchmarks/mos` panel.
  233 - Why is VieNeu not reproducible run to run, given it has no sampler? Not
  234   investigated; it affects the incumbent in production, not just this benchmark.
  235 - Would a larger `first_chunk_frames` or a short client-side buffer turn ZeroTTS's
  236   140 ms first chunk into usable gapless audio below VieNeu's clause-split figure?
  237   Untested, and it is the one change that could flip the TTFA column.
  238 - The `code-switch` subset is 9 sentences. The three- to five-fold CER gap is
  239   large enough to survive that, but it is not a rate worth quoting on its own.
benchmarks/tts-vi/results/report.md
    1 # ZeroTTS vs VieNeu-TTS v3 Turbo — Vietnamese TTS on CPU
    2 
    3 Measured 2026-09-14 on the project machine: i7-11700K, 8 physical cores,
    4 32 GB RAM, **Ubuntu** (Linux 7.0.0-31-generic), CPU only. 8 inference threads
    5 per engine, `onnxruntime` 1.27.0 pinned for both. Two run tags, arms run in
    6 opposite order in `r2`. Raw numbers: `report-speed.md` and `results/*/`.
    7 
    8 ## Verdict: **HOLD** — do not swap yet; the MOS panel decides
    9 
   10 ZeroTTS is **substantially more intelligible** and **measurably slower**. Neither
   11 difference is marginal, they point opposite ways, and the one remaining input —
   12 how the two actually *sound* — has never been measured for either engine.
   13 
   14 | Dimension | Winner | Separated? |
   15 | --------- | ------ | ---------- |
   16 | Intelligibility (conversational) | **ZeroTTS**, ~2.4× lower WER | Yes — both genders, both tags, both judges |
   17 | Intelligibility (VIVOS) | — | No — every interval spans zero |
   18 | Synthesis speed (RTF, latency/word) | **VieNeu** | Yes — ranges do not overlap |
   19 | Time to first *gapless* audio | — | No — ranges overlap |
   20 | Load time, peak RAM | **VieNeu** | Yes, but both are small absolute differences |
   21 | Licence | — | Tie; both permissive (see below) |
   22 | Naturalness | **not measured** | — |
   23 
   24 **The specific question that settles it:** in a blinded `benchmarks/mos` panel on
   25 these retained WAVs, does ZeroTTS sound at least as natural as VieNeu? If yes,
   26 swap — a 14.8% → 5.8% WER improvement is worth an RTF of 0.79 against 0.51 when
   27 both stay comfortably under real time. If it sounds worse, the trade is genuinely
   28 contested and the incumbent's advantage is real.
   29

  223 - Does ZeroTTS sound at least as natural as VieNeu? This is the only remaining
  224   input to the swap decision, and it needs the `benchmarks/mos` panel.
  225 - Why is VieNeu not reproducible run to run, given it has no sampler? Not
  226   investigated; it affects the incumbent in production, not just this benchmark.
  227 - Would a larger `first_chunk_frames` or a short client-side buffer turn ZeroTTS's
  228   140 ms first chunk into usable gapless audio below VieNeu's clause-split figure?
  229   Untested, and it is the one change that could flip the TTFA column.
  230 - The `code-switch` subset is 9 sentences. The three- to five-fold CER gap is
  231   large enough to survive that, but it is not a rate worth quoting on its own.
benchmarks/tts-vi/results/report-speed.md
    1 # Vietnamese TTS speed report — ZeroTTS vs VieNeu v3 Turbo
    2 
    3 Generated by `run_benchmark.py --render-only`. Numbers only; the verdict,
    4 the vendor-claim reconciliation and the caveats live in `report.md`.
    5 
    6 ## Measurement conditions
    7 
    8 - OS: **Linux 7.0.0-31-generic** (x86_64)
    9 - Python 3.11.15, onnxruntime 1.27.0
   10 - Threads per engine: **8**, both engines, both sessions
   11 - `HF_HUB_OFFLINE`: True — no network call inside a timed section
   12 
   13 > Every earlier measurement recorded in `docs/development-journey.md` was taken
   14 > on **Windows 11** on this same CPU. This harness runs on Linux, which is the
   15 > first thing to suspect when a figure here disagrees with one recorded there.

   17 ## Sentence set: `conversational`

   19 | arm | tag | n | p50 s | p95 s | latency/word | audio/word | RTF | load s | peak MB |
   20 | --- | --- | -: | -: | -: | -: | -: | -: | -: | -: |
   21 | vieneu-vi / mai-anh | r1 | 41 | 1.842 | 3.497 | 0.185 | 0.255 | 0.726 | 1.98 | 1542 |
   22 | vieneu-vi / mai-anh | r2 | 41 | 1.551 | 2.448 | 0.128 | 0.252 | 0.507 | 1.73 | 1500 |

   25 | zerotts-vi / baotrang | r1 | 41 | 2.105 | 3.333 | 0.175 | 0.222 | 0.789 | 4.09 | 1676 |
   26 | zerotts-vi / baotrang | r2 | 41 | 2.027 | 3.341 | 0.174 | 0.222 | 0.782 | 3.98 | 1690 |
   27 | zerotts-vi / quangminh | r1 | 41 | 1.942 | 3.108 | 0.163 | 0.207 | 0.788 | 4.00 | 1670 |
   28 | zerotts-vi / quangminh | r2 | 41 | 2.030 | 3.175 | 0.166 | 0.207 | 0.799 | 4.14 | 1677 |

   30 ### Time to first audio (median, ms)
   31 
   32 `clause-split` is what the app waits for today — it splits translated text
   33 in front of the engine. That column, not `whole`, is the incumbent baseline
   34 a streaming challenger has to beat.
   35 
   36 | arm | tag | whole | clause-split | streamed (first chunk) | underrun median | underrun worst | **gapless start** |
   37 | --- | --- | -: | -: | -: | -: | -: | -: |
   38 | vieneu-vi / mai-anh | r1 | 1842 | 1256 | — | — | — | **—** |
   39 | vieneu-vi / mai-anh | r2 | 1551 | 842 | — | — | — | **—** |
   40 | vieneu-vi / thanh-bình | r1 | 1613 | 1071 | — | — | — | **—** |
   41 | vieneu-vi / thanh-bình | r2 | 1423 | 980 | — | — | — | **—** |
   42 | zerotts-vi / baotrang | r1 | 2105 | 1144 | 140 | +766 | +939 | **911** |
   43 | zerotts-vi / baotrang | r2 | 2027 | 1112 | 138 | +798 | +924 | **938** |
   44 | zerotts-vi / quangminh | r1 | 1942 | 1028 | 138 | +768 | +983 | **910** |
   45 | zerotts-vi / quangminh | r2 | 2030 | 1077 | 140 | +777 | +1235 | **920** |
   46 
   47 A **positive** underrun margin means the stream fell behind playback: the
   48 listener ran out of audio and heard a gap. A fast first chunk with a positive
   49 margin is not a win, and reporting the first chunk alone would hide it.
   50 
   51 **`gapless start` is the column to compare against `clause-split`.** It is the
   52 first chunk plus the worst the stream then fell behind — i.e. how long a
   53 player must buffer before it can run to the end without stalling. The
   54 first-chunk figure is what a vendor quotes; this is what a listener waits for.
   55
