   156	
   157	## Success Criteria
   158	
   159	- [ ] `results/report.md` covers every arm, both sentence sets, the `code-switch` subset, and both judges with their control rows
   160	- [ ] Latency per word is the headline speed metric; RTF is labelled duration-normalized and sits beside speaking rate
   161	- [ ] WER significance uses the paired-bootstrap test, **not** the r1/r2 rule, and the report says why
   162	- [ ] A claimed-versus-measured table exists for the three comparable vendor claims
   163	- [ ] The reconciliation names Windows→Ubuntu as the first candidate cause
   164	- [ ] The verdict is stated as swap / hold / keep, with "too close to call" and "blocked on judge disagreement" both available
   165	- [ ] TTFA is weighed against **VieNeu clause-split**, not whole-sentence
   166	- [ ] All eight honesty notes appear, verified by the loop above
   167	- [ ] Both licence positions recorded
   168	- [ ] `docs/development-journey.md` carries the comparison in Vietnamese, including the OS change
   169	- [ ] `plans/reports/` carries the standalone report, unresolved questions last
   170	- [ ] `git diff` proves `services/local-tts`, `apps/api`, `benchmarks/tts`, `benchmarks/stt` are untouched
   171	
   172	## Risk and rollback
   173	
   174	The failure mode here is overclaiming: reporting a WER difference as a quality
   175	verdict, reading a gap smaller than the spread as a win, or comparing ZeroTTS
   176	streaming against an incumbent configuration the app stopped shipping. Each is
   177	guarded by a specific rule above rather than by care. Everything in this phase is
   178	documentation — rollback is reverting the edits.
     1	---
     2	title: "Benchmark — ZeroTTS vs VieNeu-TTS v3 Turbo (Vietnamese TTS, CPU)"
     3	date: 2026-09-14
     4	plan: plans/260914-1135-benchmark-zerotts-vs-vieneu/
     5	harness: benchmarks/tts-vi/
     6	verdict: HOLD — ZeroTTS wins intelligibility, VieNeu wins speed, naturalness unmeasured
     7	---
     8	
     9	# Benchmark — ZeroTTS vs VieNeu-TTS v3 Turbo (Vietnamese TTS, CPU)
    10	
    11	Measured 2026-09-14 on the project machine: i7-11700K, 8 physical cores,
    12	32 GB RAM, **Ubuntu** (Linux 7.0.0-31-generic), CPU only. 8 inference threads
    13	per engine, `onnxruntime` 1.27.0 pinned for both. Two run tags, arms run in
    14	opposite order in `r2`. Raw numbers: `report-speed.md` and `results/*/`.
    15	
    16	## Verdict: **HOLD** — do not swap yet; the MOS panel decides
    17	
    18	ZeroTTS is **substantially more intelligible** and **measurably slower**. Neither
    19	difference is marginal, they point opposite ways, and the one remaining input —
    20	how the two actually *sound* — has never been measured for either engine.
    21	
    22	| Dimension | Winner | Separated? |
    23	| --------- | ------ | ---------- |
    24	| Intelligibility (conversational) | **ZeroTTS**, ~2.4× lower WER | Yes — both genders, both tags, both judges |
    25	| Intelligibility (VIVOS) | — | No — every interval spans zero |
    26	| Synthesis speed (RTF, latency/word) | **VieNeu** | Yes — ranges do not overlap |
    27	| Time to first *gapless* audio | — | No — ranges overlap |
    28	| Load time, peak RAM | **VieNeu** | Yes, but both are small absolute differences |
    29	| Licence | — | Tie; both permissive (see below) |
    30	| Naturalness | **not measured** | — |
    31	
    32	**The specific question that settles it:** in a blinded `benchmarks/mos` panel on
    33	these retained WAVs, does ZeroTTS sound at least as natural as VieNeu? If yes,
    34	swap — a 14.8% → 5.8% WER improvement is worth an RTF of 0.79 against 0.51 when
    35	both stay comfortably under real time. If it sounds worse, the trade is genuinely
    36	contested and the incumbent's advantage is real.
    37	
    38	## Intelligibility — ZeroTTS wins, and it is not close
    39	
    40	Corpus WER %, scoring judge **PhoWhisper-small**. Control row is the same judge's
    41	WER on **human** speech over the same 50 VIVOS utterances.
    42	
    43	### Conversational set (41 sentences — the set the verdict rests on)
    44	
    45	| Arm | WER r1 | WER r2 | CER r1 | CER r2 |
    46	| --- | -: | -: | -: | -: |
    47	| zerotts / baotrang (f) | **5.75** | **6.57** | 3.43 | 3.67 |
    48	| zerotts / quangminh (m) | **6.78** | **6.78** | 3.48 | 3.48 |
    49	| vieneu / Mai Anh (f) | 14.78 | 17.25 | 11.21 | 13.86 |
    50	| vieneu / Thanh Bình (m) | 15.20 | 14.37 | 9.26 | 8.77 |
    51	| *control: human speech* | *7.71* | | | |
    52	
    53	Paired bootstrap over sentences, 10,000 resamples, ZeroTTS minus VieNeu at the
    54	same gender (negative favours ZeroTTS):
    55	
    56	| Comparison | r1 diff | r1 95% CI | r2 diff | r2 95% CI | Separates |
    57	| ---------- | -: | -: | -: | -: | --- |
    58	| conversational, female | −9.03pp | [−18.80, −1.06] | −10.68pp | [−23.31, −0.18] | **yes, both tags** |
    59	| conversational, male | −8.42pp | [−14.82, −3.64] | −7.60pp | [−13.24, −2.61] | **yes, both tags** |
    60	| vivos, female | −14.52pp | [−31.57, +0.54] | −18.64pp | [−43.94, +0.75] | no |
    61	| vivos, male | +0.54pp | [−9.48, +12.72] | −4.84pp | [−21.07, +10.02] | no |
    62	
    63	**Rank agreement.** At engine level, both judges put both ZeroTTS arms above both
    64	VieNeu arms on the conversational set, in both tags. On VIVOS they disagree in
    65	`r1` (PhoWhisper has one VieNeu arm overlapping the ZeroTTS pair) and agree in
    66	`r2` — consistent with VIVOS being inconclusive under the bootstrap anyway.
    67	
    68	**Code-switching is where the gap is widest.** CER on the 9 rows carrying
    69	embedded English, the case ZeroTTS specifically claims:
    70	
    71	| Arm | csCER r1 | csCER r2 |
    72	| --- | -: | -: |
    73	| zerotts / baotrang | **6.56** | **7.24** |
    74	| zerotts / quangminh | **7.69** | **7.69** |
    75	| vieneu / Mai Anh | 39.37 | 27.60 |
    76	| vieneu / Thanh Bình | 23.30 | 16.06 |
    77	
    78	CER is primary here rather than WER: the normalizer splits `check-in` into two
    79	tokens, so word-level scoring on nine sentences punishes tokenization as much as
    80	audio. Even allowing for that, the gap is three- to five-fold and consistent
    81	across voices and tags.
    82	
    83	### VIVOS set (50 sentences — comparability only)
    84	
    85	Both engines score far worse here than on conversational text (ZeroTTS 15.6–17.6%,
    86	VieNeu 16.9–34.2%), and no comparison separates. The set is news-register, carries
    87	proper nouns, and is redistributed inside ZeroBench-TTS — so it was never the arm
    88	the verdict rests on. Reported because it is the material the existing STT numbers
    89	were measured on.
    90	
    91	## Speed — VieNeu wins, consistently
    92	
    93	Conversational set. Ranges across both tags and both voices.
    94	
    95	| | VieNeu | ZeroTTS |
    96	| --- | -: | -: |
    97	| RTF (pooled) | **0.507 – 0.726** | 0.782 – 0.799 |
    98	| Latency per word | **0.120 – 0.185 s** | 0.163 – 0.175 s |
    99	| p50 per sentence | **1.42 – 1.84 s** | 1.94 – 2.11 s |
   100	| Load time | **1.73 – 1.98 s** | 3.98 – 4.14 s |
   101	| Peak RSS | **1425 – 1542 MB** | 1670 – 1690 MB |
   102	| Speaking rate (audio/word) | 0.231 – 0.255 s | **0.207 – 0.225 s** |
   103	
   104	The RTF ranges do not overlap, so this separates. Note the speaking-rate row:
   105	ZeroTTS talks *faster*, which makes its RTF look worse than a like-for-like
   106	comparison would — RTF is duration-normalized, so an engine producing shorter
   107	audio for the same words is penalised. Latency per word, which is not
   108	duration-normalized, still favours VieNeu at its faster end and is roughly a tie
   109	at its slower end.
   110	
   111	VieNeu's run-to-run spread is wide (RTF 0.507–0.726 for the same voice across
   112	tags) while ZeroTTS's is very tight (0.782–0.799 across everything). That matches
   113	the variance `docs/development-journey.md` already recorded for VieNeu and is the
   114	reason the two tags are reported separately rather than averaged.
   115	
   116	## Time to first audio — the vendor's headline claim, and what it is worth
   117	
   118	| Arm | first chunk | underrun | **gapless start** |
   119	| --- | -: | -: | -: |
   120	| ZeroTTS streaming | **138 – 140 ms** | +766 to +798 ms | **910 – 938 ms** |
   121	| ZeroTTS clause-split | 1028 – 1144 ms | — | 1028 – 1144 ms |
   122	| **VieNeu clause-split** (ships today) | 842 – 1256 ms | — | **842 – 1256 ms** |
   123	| VieNeu whole-sentence (context only) | 1423 – 1842 ms | — | — |
   124	
   125	**The 70 ms claim is directionally real and practically void on this machine.**
   126	ZeroTTS genuinely delivers first audio in ~140 ms. But its chunk schedule opens
   127	with a single 12.5 fps frame — 80 ms of audio — and the engine cannot generate
   128	faster than real time early in the stream, so playback runs dry. The worst
   129	shortfall is ~0.8 s. Time until audio can play *to the end without a gap* is
   130	**~911 ms**, which sits inside VieNeu's clause-split range. By the r1/r2 spread
   131	rule these have **not** separated.
   132	
   133	No stream failed outright: 0 of 41 had total generation time exceeding their audio
   134	duration, so each one catches up eventually. The deficit is entirely front-loaded.
   135	
   136	Reporting the first chunk alone would have concluded that ZeroTTS improves
   137	time-to-first-audio sevenfold. That conclusion would have been wrong.
   138	
   139	**The comparison must be against clause-split, not whole-sentence.** The app
   140	already splits translated text in front of the engine
   141	(`apps/api/src/modules/translate/audio/clause-splitter.ts`). Timing VieNeu
   142	whole-sentence and calling it the baseline would have overstated the incumbent by
   143	roughly 1.7× and manufactured a ZeroTTS win.
   144	
   145	On VIVOS, clause-split TTFA is *slower* than whole-sentence for every arm. That is
   146	correct, not a bug: those references carry no punctuation at all, so splitting is a
   147	no-op and the measurement is the whole sentence plus one extra call's overhead.
   148	
   149	## Vendor claims, reconciled
   150	
   151	| Claim | Measured here | Verdict |
   152	| ----- | ------------- | ------- |
   153	| 70 ms to first audio sample | 138–140 ms first chunk; **911 ms to gapless** | Partly — real as a first chunk, not as usable audio |
   154	| RTF 0.50× | **0.78–0.80**, very consistent | Not reproduced |
   155	| WER 1.03% | 5.75–6.78% (conversational), 15.6–17.6% (VIVOS) | **Not comparable** — see below |
   156	| UTMOSv2 2.91 | deliberately not measured | — |
   157	
   158	The WER row cannot be compared. The vendor used PhoWhisper-**large** together
   159	with whisper-large-v3 and took the per-utterance minimum; this is
   160	PhoWhisper-**small** alone. Absolute WER here is higher for *both* engines by
   161	construction. The figure is only meaningful as a comparison between the two
   162	engines on identical sentences — which is what it is used for.
   163	
   164	The vendor named no CPU alongside its figures, so the RTF gap could be hardware.
   165	This machine is an i7-11700K at 8 physical cores.

benchmarks/tts-vi/run_benchmark.py
    1 """Orchestrate every arm, then refuse to call an incomplete run complete.
    2 
    3     uv run python run_benchmark.py --run-tag r1 --sentences data/sentences-conversational.jsonl
    4     uv run python run_benchmark.py --check-complete
    5     uv run python run_benchmark.py --render-only --report-out results/report-speed.md
    6 
    7 Three things this does that the harness it was vendored from does not:
    8 
    9 **Rendering a report does not re-measure.** In the original, `--report-out`
   10 re-runs every engine, which here would overwrite the very WAVs the scoring stage
   11 is about to transcribe. `--render-only` reads what is on disk.
   12 
   13 **Arm order alternates between run tags.** A fixed order lets thermal and
   14 frequency drift land on the same arm in both tags, where it is invisible to any
   15 r1-versus-r2 comparison and reads as a property of the engine.
   16 
   17 **`--check-complete` is a gate, not a listing.** A failed arm may not abort the
   18 others — but the run must not be reportable as though nothing was missing.
   19 """
   20 
   21 import argparse
   22 import itertools
   23 import json
   24 import os
   25 import subprocess
   26 import sys
   27 from pathlib import Path
   28 
   29 from tts_vi_bench.engines.vieneu_vi import VieNeuVi
   30 from tts_vi_bench.engines.zerotts_vi import ZeroTtsVi
   31 from tts_vi_bench.run_engine import voice_slug
   32 from tts_vi_bench.measure import sentence_set_name
   33 
   34 BENCH_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
   35 RESULTS = BENCH_ROOT / "results"
   36 ENGINES = [VieNeuVi, ZeroTtsVi]
   37 SENTENCE_SETS = ["conversational", "vivos"]
   38 RUN_TAGS = ["r1", "r2"]
   39 
   40 #: Streaming is measured on the conversational set only. It costs a full extra
   41 #: synthesis per sentence, and VIVOS is the comparability arm — the verdict
   42 #: rests on the conversational one, which is also the register the app emits.
   43 STREAM_SETS = {"conversational"}
   44 
   45 
   46 def arms():
   47     """Every (engine, gender, voice) the benchmark measures."""
   48     for cls in ENGINES:
   49         for gender, voice in cls.VOICES.items():
   50             yield cls.engine_id, gender, voice
   51 
   52 
   53 def run_tag(tag: str, sentences_path: Path, force: bool) -> list[str]:
   54     set_name = sentence_set_name(sentences_path)
   55     out_dir = RESULTS / tag
   56     ordered = list(arms())
   57     # r2 runs the arms in reverse, so drift does not always favour the same one.
   58     if RUN_TAGS.index(tag) % 2 == 1:
   59         ordered.reverse()
   60 
   61     failed = []
   62     for index, (engine_id, gender, voice) in enumerate(ordered):
   63         print(f"=== {tag} [{index}] {engine_id} / {voice} / {set_name} ===", flush=True)
   64         cmd = [
   65             sys.executable, "-m", "tts_vi_bench.run_engine",
   66             "--engine", engine_id, "--voice", voice, "--gender", gender,
   67             "--sentences", str(sentences_path), "--out-dir", str(out_dir),
   68         ]
   69         if force:
   70             cmd.append("--force")
   71         if set_name not in STREAM_SETS:
   72             cmd.append("--no-stream")
   73         if subprocess.run(cmd, cwd=BENCH_ROOT).returncode != 0:
   74             print(f"[warn] {engine_id}/{voice}/{set_name} failed; continuing", flush=True)
   75             failed.append(f"{tag}:{engine_id}:{voice_slug(voice)}:{set_name}")
   76     return failed
   77 
   78 
   79 def check_complete() -> int:
   80     """Every arm x set x tag present, and every file as long as it promised."""
   81     problems = []
   82     for tag, set_name in itertools.product(RUN_TAGS, SENTENCE_SETS):
   83         for engine_id, _gender, voice in arms():
   84             path = RESULTS / tag / f"{engine_id}__{voice_slug(voice)}__{set_name}.jsonl"
   85             if not path.exists():
   86                 problems.append(f"MISSING {path.relative_to(BENCH_ROOT)}")
   87                 continue
   88             rows = [json.loads(l) for l in path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines() if l.strip()]
   89             header = next((r for r in rows if r["type"] == "header"), None)
   90             footer = next((r for r in rows if r["type"] == "footer"), None)
   91             n = sum(1 for r in rows if r["type"] == "sentence")
   92             if header is None:
   93                 problems.append(f"NO HEADER {path.name}")
   94                 continue
   95             expected = header["num_sentences_expected"]
   96             if n != expected:
   97                 problems.append(f"SHORT {path.name}: {n}/{expected} sentences")
   98             if footer is None:
   99                 problems.append(f"NO FOOTER {path.name} (arm died before finishing)")
  100             wavs = list((RESULTS / tag / "wav" / engine_id / voice_slug(voice) / set_name).glob("*.wav"))
  101             if len(wavs) != expected:
  102                 problems.append(
  103                     f"WAVS {engine_id}/{voice_slug(voice)}/{set_name}@{tag}: "
  104                     f"{len(wavs)}/{expected}"
  105                 )
  106     if problems:
  107         print("INCOMPLETE:", file=sys.stderr)
  108         for p in problems:
  109             print(f"  {p}", file=sys.stderr)
  110         return 1
  111     print(f"complete: {len(RUN_TAGS)} tags x {len(SENTENCE_SETS)} sets x "
  112           f"{len(list(arms()))} arms, all full length")
  113     return 0
  114 
  115 
  116 def main() -> int:
  117     parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
  118     parser.add_argument("--run-tag", choices=RUN_TAGS)
  119     parser.add_argument("--sentences", type=Path)
  120     parser.add_argument("--all", action="store_true",
  121                         help="every tag x sentence set, in order")
  122     parser.add_argument("--force", action="store_true")
  123     parser.add_argument("--check-complete", action="store_true")
  124     parser.add_argument("--render-only", action="store_true")
  125     parser.add_argument("--report-out", type=Path)
  126     args = parser.parse_args()
  127 
  128     if args.check_complete:
  129         return check_complete()
  130 
  131     failed: list[str] = []
  132     if not args.render_only:
  133         if args.all:
  134             for tag, set_name in itertools.product(RUN_TAGS, SENTENCE_SETS):
  135                 failed += run_tag(tag, BENCH_ROOT / "data" / f"sentences-{set_name}.jsonl",
  136                                   args.force)
  137         elif args.run_tag and args.sentences:
  138             failed = run_tag(args.run_tag, args.sentences, args.force)
  139         elif not args.report_out:
  140             parser.error("need --all, or --run-tag with --sentences, or --render-only")
  141 
  142     if args.report_out:
  143         from tts_vi_bench.report import render_report
  144 
  145         args.report_out.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
  146         args.report_out.write_text(render_report(RESULTS), encoding="utf-8")
  147         print(f"[done] report -> {args.report_out}")
  148 
  149     if failed:
  150         print(f"[warn] failed arms: {failed}", file=sys.stderr)
  151         return 1
  152     return 0
  153 
  154 
  155 if __name__ == "__main__":
  156     sys.exit(main())
benchmarks/tts-vi/score_intelligibility.py
    1 """Transcribe every synthesized WAV and score it — the intelligibility stage.
    2 
    3     uv run python score_intelligibility.py --run-tag r1
    4     uv run python score_intelligibility.py --check-complete
    5 
    6 **The scoring judge is PhoWhisper-small. Zipformer-vi is a separate column, and
    7 the two are never combined** — see `tts_vi_bench/asr_judges.py` for why a
    8 min-of-two would move the between-system delta in an unpredictable direction and
    9 would throw away the human-speech control floors.
   10 
   11 WHAT THIS NUMBER IS NOT: the vendor's 1.03% WER was measured with
   12 PhoWhisper-LARGE plus whisper-large-v3, taking the per-utterance minimum. This
   13 is PhoWhisper-small alone. Absolute WER here will be higher for BOTH engines and
   14 is meaningful only as a comparison between them. It is not comparable to 1.03%,
   15 and the generated report says so on every table rather than only in the plan.
   16 
   17 Completeness is a gate, not a listing: the arms are joined to the sentence set by
   18 id and a mismatch fails, because a corpus WER computed over whatever WAVs
   19 happened to exist is a plausible-looking number with nothing marking it as
   20 partial.
   21 """
   22 
   23 import argparse
   24 import json
   25 import sys
   26 from pathlib import Path
   27 
   28 from tts_vi_bench.asr_judges import (
   29     HUMAN_SPEECH_FLOOR_WER,
   30     JUDGES,
   31     SCORING_JUDGE,
   32 )
   33 from tts_vi_bench.measure import load_sentences
   34 from tts_vi_bench.metrics import (
   35     corpus_cer,
   36     corpus_wer,
   37     paired_bootstrap_wer,
   38     win_loss_tie,
   39 )
   40 from tts_vi_bench.run_engine import voice_slug
   41 from run_benchmark import RESULTS, RUN_TAGS, SENTENCE_SETS, arms
   42 
   43 BENCH_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
   44 
   45 
   46 def sentences_for(set_name: str):
   47     return load_sentences(BENCH_ROOT / "data" / f"sentences-{set_name}.jsonl")
   48 
   49 
   50 def wav_dir(tag: str, engine_id: str, voice: str, set_name: str) -> Path:
   51     return RESULTS / tag / "wav" / engine_id / voice_slug(voice) / set_name
   52 
   53 
   54 def check_complete() -> int:
   55     problems = []
   56     for tag in RUN_TAGS:
   57         for set_name in SENTENCE_SETS:
   58             expected = {s.id for s in sentences_for(set_name)}
   59             for engine_id, _gender, voice in arms():
   60                 d = wav_dir(tag, engine_id, voice, set_name)
   61                 found = {p.stem for p in d.glob("*.wav")} if d.exists() else set()
   62                 if found != expected:
   63                     problems.append(
   64                         f"{tag}/{engine_id}/{voice_slug(voice)}/{set_name}: "
   65                         f"{len(found)}/{len(expected)} wavs"
   66                         + (f", missing {sorted(expected - found)[:3]}..." if expected - found else "")
   67                     )
   68     if problems:
   69         print("INCOMPLETE — refusing to score a partial run:", file=sys.stderr)
   70         for p in problems:
   71             print(f"  {p}", file=sys.stderr)
   72         return 1
   73     print("complete: every arm has a WAV for every sentence in its set")
   74     return 0
   75 
   76 
   77 def transcribe_all(tag: str) -> dict:
   78     """{judge: {(engine, voice_slug, set): {sentence_id: hypothesis}}}"""
   79     out: dict = {}
   80     for judge_id, judge_cls in JUDGES.items():
   81         judge = judge_cls()
   82         judge.load()
   83         out[judge_id] = {}
   84         for set_name in SENTENCE_SETS:
   85             ids = [s.id for s in sentences_for(set_name)]
   86             for engine_id, _gender, voice in arms():
   87                 d = wav_dir(tag, engine_id, voice, set_name)
   88                 key = (engine_id, voice_slug(voice), set_name)
   89                 out[judge_id][key] = {
   90                     sid: judge.transcribe(d / f"{sid}.wav") for sid in ids
   91                 }
   92                 print(f"[{judge_id}] {tag} {key} done", file=sys.stderr, flush=True)
   93     return out
   94 
   95 
   96 def score_subset(sentences, hyps: dict, tag_filter=None) -> dict:
   97     rows = [s for s in sentences if tag_filter is None or tag_filter in s.tags]
   98     if not rows:
   99         return {}
  100     refs = [s.ref_text for s in rows]
  101     hyp = [hyps[s.id] for s in rows]
  102     return {"n": len(rows), "wer": corpus_wer(refs, hyp), "cer": corpus_cer(refs, hyp)}
  103 
  104 
  105 def main() -> int:
  106     parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
  107     parser.add_argument("--run-tag", choices=RUN_TAGS)
  108     parser.add_argument("--check-complete", action="store_true")
  109     parser.add_argument("--out", type=Path)
  110     args = parser.parse_args()
  111 
  112     if args.check_complete:
  113         return check_complete()
  114     if not args.run_tag:
  115         parser.error("need --run-tag or --check-complete")
  116     if check_complete() != 0:
  117         return 1
  118 
  119     tag = args.run_tag
  120     hyps = transcribe_all(tag)
  121 
  122     per_sentence_path = RESULTS / tag / "intelligibility.jsonl"
  123     scores: dict = {}
  124     with open(per_sentence_path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
  125         f.write(json.dumps({
  126             "type": "header", "run_tag": tag,
  127             "scoring_judge": SCORING_JUDGE,
  128             "judges": {jid: JUDGES[jid]().decode_params() for jid in JUDGES},
  129             "human_speech_floor_wer": HUMAN_SPEECH_FLOOR_WER,
  130             "caveat": (
  131                 "PhoWhisper-SMALL scores here. The ZeroTTS vendor used "
  132                 "PhoWhisper-large + whisper-large-v3 taking the per-utterance "
  133                 "minimum, so absolute WER below is higher for BOTH engines and "
  134                 "is NOT comparable to their 1.03%. Read it as a relative "
  135                 "comparison between the two engines on identical sentences. "
  136                 "WER measures intelligibility, not naturalness."
  137             ),
  138         }, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
  139 
  140         for set_name in SENTENCE_SETS:
  141             sentences = sentences_for(set_name)
  142             by_id = {s.id: s for s in sentences}
  143             for engine_id, _gender, voice in arms():
  144                 key = (engine_id, voice_slug(voice), set_name)
  145                 for sid, s in by_id.items():
  146                     f.write(json.dumps({
  147                         "type": "sentence", "run_tag": tag,
  148                         "engine": engine_id, "voice_slug": key[1],
  149                         "sentence_set": set_name, "sentence_id": sid,
  150                         "tags": list(s.tags), "reference": s.ref_text,
  151                         **{f"hyp_{jid}": hyps[jid][key][sid] for jid in JUDGES},
  152                     }, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
  153 
  154                 for judge_id in JUDGES:
  155                     h = hyps[judge_id][key]
  156                     scores[(judge_id, *key)] = {
  157                         "all": score_subset(sentences, h),
  158                         "code_switch": score_subset(sentences, h, "code-switch"),
  159                     }
  160 
  161     summary_path = RESULTS / tag / "intelligibility-summary.json"
  162     summary = {
  163         "run_tag": tag,
  164         "scoring_judge": SCORING_JUDGE,
  165         "human_speech_floor_wer": HUMAN_SPEECH_FLOOR_WER,
  166         "scores": {"|".join(k): v for k, v in scores.items()},
  167     }
  168 
  169     # Paired bootstrap, scoring judge only, per sentence set: engine vs engine
  170     # at the same gender, so the comparison is not confounded by speaker.
  171     paired = {}
  172     for set_name in SENTENCE_SETS:
  173         sentences = sentences_for(set_name)
  174         refs = [s.ref_text for s in sentences]
  175         for gender in ("female", "male"):
  176             keys = {}
  177             for engine_id, g, voice in arms():
  178                 if g == gender:
  179                     keys[engine_id] = (engine_id, voice_slug(voice), set_name)
  180             if len(keys) != 2:
  181                 continue
  182             a_id, b_id = "zerotts-vi", "vieneu-vi"
  183             ha = [hyps[SCORING_JUDGE][keys[a_id]][s.id] for s in sentences]
  184             hb = [hyps[SCORING_JUDGE][keys[b_id]][s.id] for s in sentences]
  185             paired[f"{set_name}|{gender}|zerotts_vs_vieneu"] = {
  186                 **paired_bootstrap_wer(refs, ha, hb),
  187                 **win_loss_tie(refs, ha, hb),
  188                 "note": "negative observed_diff favours zerotts; "
  189                         "an interval spanning zero is a tie",
  190             }
  191     summary["paired_bootstrap"] = paired
  192     summary_path.write_text(json.dumps(summary, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
  193 
  194     print(f"[done] {per_sentence_path}")
  195     print(f"[done] {summary_path}")
  196     return 0
  197 
  198 
  199 if __name__ == "__main__":
  200     sys.exit(main())

codex
The report’s headline says latency per word separates the engines, but its own table shows overlapping ranges. I’m checking whether the “gapless” comparison also applies the same standard to both engines, and how much of the WER result depends on sentence and seed selection.

benchmarks/tts-vi/tts_vi_bench/run_engine.py
    1 """Measure one arm — one (engine, voice, sentence-set) — in its own process.
    2 
    3 Run via `run_benchmark.py`, which spawns this per arm so peak RSS reflects one
    4 model and the arms never contend for the same cores.
    5 
    6 **Result files are named for all three axes**, `{engine}__{voice}__{set}.jsonl`,
    7 and the runner refuses to overwrite one without `--force`. The harness this was
    8 vendored from names its output for the engine alone, which was unambiguous when
    9 it measured one voice over one sentence set and is silent data loss here: the
   10 second set's pass would overwrite the first's, leaving a perfectly well-formed
   11 file containing half the run.
   12 
   13 **The header is written first**, carrying the *expected* sentence count, and
   14 every row is flushed as it is produced. The original writes its header after the
   15 loop, so an arm that dies midway leaves WAVs on disk and no JSONL at all — and
   16 the scoring stage, which walks the WAV tree, would then score a truncated subset
   17 as though it were the whole set.
   18 
   19 Each sentence is measured three ways in one pass, because two of the three are
   20 nearly free once the engine is loaded:
   21 
   22 - **whole** — the headline latency, and also the time-to-first-audio of an
   23   engine that cannot stream, since there is nothing to hear until it finishes.
   24 - **clause-split first part** — what the app actually waits for today. This is
   25   the TTFA number the verdict must beat, not the whole-sentence one.
   26 - **streamed** — first audio-carrying chunk, plus the underrun margin, for an
   27   engine that streams.
   28 """
   29 
   30 import argparse
   31 import json
   32 import platform
   33 import sys
   34 import time
   35 from pathlib import Path
   36 
   37 import numpy as np
   38 import soundfile as sf
   39 
   40 from .clause_split import split_into_clauses
   41 from .engines.vieneu_vi import VieNeuVi
   42 from .engines.zerotts_vi import ZeroTtsVi
   43 from .measure import (
   44     PeakRssSampler,
   45     bench_threads,
   46     load_sentences,
   47     sentence_set_name,
   48 )
   49 
   50 ENGINES = {cls.engine_id: cls for cls in (VieNeuVi, ZeroTtsVi)}
   51 
   52 
   53 def voice_slug(voice: str) -> str:
   54     """`Mai Anh` -> `mai-anh`; used in filenames and directory names."""
   55     return "".join(c if c.isalnum() else "-" for c in voice.lower()).strip("-")
   56 
   57 
   58 def environment_block() -> dict:
   59     """What the numbers are conditional on.
   60 
   61     Recorded per arm because every earlier measurement in this repo was taken on
   62     Windows 11 and this machine is Ubuntu — a reader comparing against those
   63     figures needs to see the difference on the artifact, not infer it.
   64     """
   65     import importlib.metadata as md
   66 
   67     return {
   68         "os": platform.system(),
   69         "os_release": platform.release(),
   70         "machine": platform.machine(),
   71         "python": platform.python_version(),
   72         "onnxruntime": md.version("onnxruntime"),
   73         "threads": bench_threads(),
   74         "hf_hub_offline": bool(int(__import__("os").environ.get("HF_HUB_OFFLINE", "0") or 0)),
   75     }
   76 
   77 
   78 def measure_stream(engine, text: str, voice: str) -> dict:
   79     """Time the first audio-carrying chunk, and how far the stream stays ahead.
   80 
   81     Playback starts when the FIRST chunk lands, not when the call begins, so
   82     what the listener has consumed by wall time `t` is `t - ttfa`. The stream has
   83     delivered `cumulative_audio` by then. The worst value of
   84     `(t - ttfa) - cumulative_audio` over the stream is the underrun margin:
   85     positive anywhere means the generator fell behind the listener and the
   86     audio stalled.
   87 
   88     Measuring from `t0` instead would charge the stream for its own
   89     time-to-first-audio twice and report a stall that did not happen.
   90 
   91     A fast first chunk followed by a chunk generated slower than real time is an
   92     audible gap that a bare TTFA figure would still report as a win, and this
   93     repo already holds itself to the opposite standard: the app's clause
   94     splitter records that the first part's audio outlasts the time to synthesize
   95     the second, "so playback runs gapless".
   96     """
   97     sr = engine.sample_rate
   98     t0 = time.perf_counter()
   99     ttfa_s = None
  100     total_samples = 0
  101     worst_margin = float("-inf")
  102     chunks = []
  103 
  104     for chunk in engine.synthesize_stream(text, voice):
  105         now = time.perf_counter() - t0
  106         if chunk.size == 0:
  107             continue
  108         if ttfa_s is None:
  109             ttfa_s = now
  110         else:
  111             # Just before this chunk lands: consumed vs delivered so far.
  112             consumed_s = now - ttfa_s
  113             delivered_s = total_samples / sr
  114             worst_margin = max(worst_margin, consumed_s - delivered_s)
  115         total_samples += chunk.size
  116         chunks.append(chunk)
  117 
  118     return {
  119         "stream_ttfa_s": round(ttfa_s, 4) if ttfa_s is not None else None,
  120         "stream_total_s": round(time.perf_counter() - t0, 4),
  121         "stream_chunks": len(chunks),
  122         "stream_underrun_margin_s": (
  123             round(worst_margin, 4) if worst_margin != float("-inf") else None
  124         ),
  125         "stream_audio_s": round(total_samples / sr, 3),
  126     }
  127 
  128 
  129 def main() -> int:
  130     parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
  131     parser.add_argument("--engine", required=True, choices=sorted(ENGINES))
  132     parser.add_argument("--voice", required=True, help="voice token, verbatim")
  133     parser.add_argument("--gender", required=True, choices=["female", "male"])
  134     parser.add_argument("--sentences", required=True, type=Path)
  135     parser.add_argument("--out-dir", required=True, type=Path)
  136     parser.add_argument("--force", action="store_true",
  137                         help="overwrite an existing result file for this arm")
  138     parser.add_argument("--no-stream", action="store_true",
  139                         help="skip the streaming pass even where supported")
  140     args = parser.parse_args()
  141 
  142     sentences = load_sentences(args.sentences)
  143     set_name = sentence_set_name(args.sentences)
  144     slug = voice_slug(args.voice)
  145 
  146     out_path = args.out_dir / f"{args.engine}__{slug}__{set_name}.jsonl"
  147     if out_path.exists() and not args.force:
  148         print(f"[error] {out_path} exists; pass --force to overwrite", file=sys.stderr)
  149         return 2
  150     wav_dir = args.out_dir / "wav" / args.engine / slug / set_name
  151     wav_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
  152     out_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
  153 
  154     engine = ENGINES[args.engine]()
  155     sampler = PeakRssSampler().start()
  156 
  157     load_start = time.perf_counter()
  158     engine.load()
  159     load_s = time.perf_counter() - load_start
  160 
  161     # One untimed warm-up, on the SAME voice as the timed loop — a different
  162     # voice here would leave a per-voice initialization cost inside the first
  163     # timed sentence.
  164     engine.synthesize(sentences[0].text, args.voice)
  165 
  166     f = open(out_path, "w", encoding="utf-8")
  167     header = {
  168         "type": "header",
  169         "engine": args.engine,
  170         "voice": args.voice,
  171         "voice_slug": slug,
  172         "gender": args.gender,
  173         "sentence_set": set_name,
  174         "load_s": round(load_s, 3),
  175         # The EXPECTED count, written before the loop, so a file that ends early
  176         # is detectably short rather than plausibly complete.
  177         "num_sentences_expected": len(sentences),
  178         "sample_rate": engine.sample_rate,
  179         "supports_streaming": engine.supports_streaming and not args.no_stream,
  180         "decode_params": engine.decode_params(),
  181         "environment": environment_block(),
  182     }
  183     f.write(json.dumps(header, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
  184     f.flush()
  185 
  186     for sentence in sentences:
  187         t0 = time.perf_counter()
  188         samples, sr = engine.synthesize(sentence.text, args.voice)
  189         proc_s = time.perf_counter() - t0
  190         audio_s = samples.size / sr
  191 
  192         sf.write(wav_dir / f"{sentence.id}.wav", samples, sr, subtype="PCM_16")
  193 
  194         # What the app waits for today: the first clause only.
  195         parts = split_into_clauses(sentence.text)
  196         t0 = time.perf_counter()
  197         engine.synthesize(parts[0], args.voice)
  198         clause_ttfa_s = time.perf_counter() - t0
  199 
  200         n_words = len(sentence.text.split())
  201         record = {
  202             "type": "sentence",
  203             "engine": args.engine,
  204             "voice_slug": slug,
  205             "sentence_set": set_name,
  206             "sentence_id": sentence.id,
  207             "text": sentence.text,
  208             "tags": list(sentence.tags),
  209             "n_words": n_words,
  210             "n_clauses": len(parts),
  211             "audio_s": round(audio_s, 3),
  212             "proc_s": round(proc_s, 4),
  213             "rtf": round(proc_s / audio_s, 4) if audio_s > 0 else None,
  214             # Speaking rate. RTF is invariant to sample rate but NOT to how fast
  215             # an engine talks: render the same sentence 20% longer and RTF
  216             # improves 20% while the user waits longer to synthesize AND longer
  217             # to listen. Recorded so the report can say whether an RTF gap is
  218             # between engines or between prosody choices.
  219             "audio_s_per_word": round(audio_s / n_words, 4) if n_words else None,
  220             "latency_s_per_word": round(proc_s / n_words, 4) if n_words else None,
  221             # TTFA, all applicable ways.
  222             "ttfa_whole_s": round(proc_s, 4),
  223             "ttfa_clause_split_s": round(clause_ttfa_s, 4),
  224         }
  225         if engine.supports_streaming and not args.no_stream:
  226             record.update(measure_stream(engine, sentence.text, args.voice))
  227 
  228         f.write(json.dumps(record, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
  229         f.flush()
  230         print(f"[{args.engine}/{slug}/{set_name}] {sentence.id}: "
  231               f"{proc_s:.2f}s -> {audio_s:.1f}s audio", file=sys.stderr, flush=True)
  232 
  233     peak_rss_mb = sampler.stop()
  234     f.write(json.dumps({"type": "footer", "peak_rss_mb": round(peak_rss_mb, 1),
  235                         "num_sentences_written": len(sentences)},
  236                        ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
  237     f.close()
  238     print(f"[{args.engine}/{slug}/{set_name}] wrote {out_path}", file=sys.stderr)
  239     return 0
  240 
  241 
  242 if __name__ == "__main__":
  243     sys.exit(main())
benchmarks/tts-vi/tts_vi_bench/measure.py
    1 """Measurement utilities — latency stats, peak-RSS sampling, sentence loading.
    2 
    3 Self-contained copies of the patterns proven in benchmarks/tts (which in turn
    4 copied them from benchmarks/stt): the benchmark projects stay independent on
    5 purpose so any one can be deleted or evolved without breaking the others.
    6 
    7 One deliberate divergence from the copy in benchmarks/tts: `load_sentences`
    8 reads JSONL and takes each row's own `id`, where the original parses plain text
    9 and assigns ids positionally. That harness measured one sentence set, so
   10 positional ids were unambiguous. This one measures two, into one results tree —
   11 positional ids would make conversational `s001` and VIVOS `s001` collide on
   12 filename and silently overwrite each other's audio.
   13 """
   14 
   15 import json
   16 import os
   17 import threading
   18 import time
   19 from dataclasses import dataclass
   20 from pathlib import Path
   21 
   22 import numpy as np
   23 import psutil
   24 
   25 BENCH_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
   26 MODELS_DIR = BENCH_ROOT / "models"
   27 
   28 
   29 def bench_threads() -> int:
   30     """CPU threads per engine; TTS_VI_BENCH_THREADS env, default 8.
   31 
   32     8 is the physical core count of the measurement machine. The sidecar records
   33     that 8 beat 16 hyperthreads there, and matching it is what lets these
   34     numbers transfer to the running service.
   35     """
   36     return int(os.environ.get("TTS_VI_BENCH_THREADS", "8"))
   37 
   38 
   39 def latency_stats(samples_seconds: list[float]) -> dict[str, float]:
   40     """Mean / p50 / p95 over per-sentence synthesis times (seconds)."""
   41     if not samples_seconds:
   42         raise ValueError("no latency samples")
   43     arr = np.asarray(samples_seconds, dtype=np.float64)
   44     return {
   45         "mean_s": float(arr.mean()),
   46         "p50_s": float(np.percentile(arr, 50)),
   47         "p95_s": float(np.percentile(arr, 95)),
   48     }
   49 
   50 
   51 class PeakRssSampler:
   52     """Samples process-tree RSS in a background thread, records the peak."""
   53 
   54     def __init__(self, pid: int | None = None, interval_s: float = 0.1):
   55         self._process = psutil.Process(pid)
   56         self._interval_s = interval_s
   57         self._peak_bytes = 0
   58         self._stop_event = threading.Event()
   59         self._thread = threading.Thread(target=self._sample_loop, daemon=True)
   60 
   61     def _tree_rss(self) -> int:
   62         total = 0
   63         try:
   64             total = self._process.memory_info().rss
   65             for child in self._process.children(recursive=True):
   66                 try:
   67                     total += child.memory_info().rss
   68                 except psutil.NoSuchProcess:
   69                     continue
   70         except psutil.NoSuchProcess:
   71             pass
   72         return total
   73 
   74     def _sample_loop(self) -> None:
   75         while not self._stop_event.is_set():
   76             self._peak_bytes = max(self._peak_bytes, self._tree_rss())
   77             time.sleep(self._interval_s)
   78 
   79     def start(self) -> "PeakRssSampler":
   80         self._peak_bytes = self._tree_rss()
   81         self._thread.start()
   82         return self
   83 
   84     def stop(self) -> float:
   85         """Stop sampling; returns peak RSS in MB."""
   86         self._stop_event.set()
   87         self._thread.join(timeout=2)
   88         self._peak_bytes = max(self._peak_bytes, self._tree_rss())
   89         return self._peak_bytes / (1024 * 1024)
   90 
   91 
   92 @dataclass(frozen=True)
   93 class Sentence:
   94     """One row of a sentence set.
   95 
   96     `text` is synthesized; `ref_text` is what the ASR transcript is scored
   97     against. They differ only where the set was built from a corpus whose
   98     reference casing is not what we want to feed an engine — VIVOS is ALL CAPS.
   99     """
  100 
  101     id: str
  102     text: str
  103     ref_text: str
  104     tags: tuple[str, ...]
  105 
  106 
  107 def load_sentences(path: Path) -> list[Sentence]:
  108     """Load a JSONL sentence set, taking each row's declared `id`.
  109 
  110     Blank lines and `#` comment lines are skipped, so a set can carry its policy
  111     header in the file itself rather than only in the plan.
  112     """
  113     sentences: list[Sentence] = []
  114     seen: set[str] = set()
  115     with open(path, encoding="utf-8") as f:
  116         for lineno, line in enumerate(f, 1):
  117             line = line.strip()
  118             if not line or line.startswith("#"):
  119                 continue
  120             row = json.loads(line)
  121             sid = row["id"]
  122             if sid in seen:
  123                 raise ValueError(f"{path}:{lineno}: duplicate sentence id {sid!r}")
  124             seen.add(sid)
  125             if not row["text"].strip() or not row["ref_text"].strip():
  126                 raise ValueError(f"{path}:{lineno}: empty text or ref_text for {sid!r}")
  127             sentences.append(
  128                 Sentence(
  129                     id=sid,
  130                     text=row["text"],
  131                     ref_text=row["ref_text"],
  132                     tags=tuple(row.get("tags", ())),
  133                 )
  134             )
  135     if not sentences:
  136         raise ValueError(f"no sentences in {path}")
  137     return sentences
  138 
  139 
  140 def sentence_set_name(path: Path) -> str:
  141     """`data/sentences-vivos.jsonl` -> `vivos`; used in result filenames."""
  142     stem = Path(path).stem
  143     return stem[len("sentences-"):] if stem.startswith("sentences-") else stem
benchmarks/tts-vi/tts_vi_bench/metrics.py
    1 """Corpus WER and CER, vendored from `benchmarks/stt/stt_bench/metrics.py`.
    2 
    3 Same functions, same normalizer, so a number here sits beside the STT harness's
    4 numbers without an asterisk — which is what makes the human-speech control floors
    5 in `docs/development-journey.md` applicable to this harness at all.
    6 
    7 Added here and not in the STT copy: a paired bootstrap over sentences. The
    8 r1-versus-r2 spread is the right significance guard for latency, and a vacuous
    9 one for WER — synthesis is seeded and the ASR decodes greedily, so a second run
   10 transcribes bit-identical audio and any gap at all would read as clean
   11 separation. The real uncertainty is which sentences happen to be in the set.
   12 """
   13 
   14 import jiwer
   15 import numpy as np
   16 
   17 from .text_normalize import normalize_text
   18 
   19 
   20 def corpus_wer(references: list[str], hypotheses: list[str]) -> float:
   21     """Corpus-level WER over normalized ref/hyp pairs (0.0-1.0+)."""
   22     if len(references) != len(hypotheses):
   23         raise ValueError(f"ref/hyp count mismatch: {len(references)} vs {len(hypotheses)}")
   24     return jiwer.wer([normalize_text(r) for r in references],
   25                      [normalize_text(h) for h in hypotheses])
   26 
   27 
   28 def corpus_cer(references: list[str], hypotheses: list[str]) -> float:
   29     """Corpus-level CER over normalized ref/hyp pairs (0.0-1.0+).
   30 
   31     Reported alongside WER because Vietnamese carries meaning in diacritics that
   32     WER cannot resolve: a hypothesis differing from its reference by one tone
   33     mark loses the whole word to WER, exactly as an unrelated word would. CER
   34     separates a near-miss from a miss, and is the metric to read on vi.
   35     """
   36     if len(references) != len(hypotheses):
   37         raise ValueError(f"ref/hyp count mismatch: {len(references)} vs {len(hypotheses)}")
   38     return jiwer.cer([normalize_text(r) for r in references],
   39                      [normalize_text(h) for h in hypotheses])
   40 
   41 
   42 def sentence_errors(reference: str, hypothesis: str) -> tuple[int, int]:
   43     """(edit distance in words, reference word count) for one pair.
   44 
   45     Corpus WER is the ratio of the summed numerators to the summed
   46     denominators, not the mean of per-sentence rates, so the bootstrap has to
   47     resample these pairs rather than resampling rates.
   48     """
   49     ref, hyp = normalize_text(reference), normalize_text(hypothesis)
   50     n_ref = len(ref.split())
   51     if n_ref == 0:
   52         return 0, 0
   53     out = jiwer.process_words([ref], [hyp])
   54     return out.substitutions + out.deletions + out.insertions, n_ref
   55 
   56 
   57 def paired_bootstrap_wer(
   58     references: list[str],
   59     hyp_a: list[str],
   60     hyp_b: list[str],
   61     n_resamples: int = 10_000,
   62     seed: int = 20260914,
   63 ) -> dict:
   64     """Is A's corpus WER really below B's, or is it the sentence sample?
   65 
   66     Paired: each resample draws the same sentence indices for both systems, so
   67     the comparison is not contaminated by one system happening to be scored on
   68     easier sentences than the other.
   69 
   70     Returns the observed difference (A - B, so negative favours A), a 95%
   71     interval on it, and the share of resamples in which A won. An interval
   72     spanning zero is a tie, and must be reported as one.
   73     """
   74     if not (len(references) == len(hyp_a) == len(hyp_b)):
   75         raise ValueError("references and both hypothesis lists must be the same length")
   76 
   77     pairs_a = np.array([sentence_errors(r, h) for r, h in zip(references, hyp_a)], dtype=float)
   78     pairs_b = np.array([sentence_errors(r, h) for r, h in zip(references, hyp_b)], dtype=float)
   79 
   80     def wer_of(pairs, idx):
   81         denom = pairs[idx, 1].sum()
   82         return float(pairs[idx, 0].sum() / denom) if denom else float("nan")
   83 
   84     all_idx = np.arange(len(references))
   85     observed = wer_of(pairs_a, all_idx) - wer_of(pairs_b, all_idx)
   86 
   87     rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
   88     diffs = np.empty(n_resamples)
   89     for i in range(n_resamples):
   90         idx = rng.integers(0, len(references), len(references))
   91         diffs[i] = wer_of(pairs_a, idx) - wer_of(pairs_b, idx)
   92 
   93     lo, hi = np.percentile(diffs, [2.5, 97.5])
   94     return {
   95         "observed_diff": observed,
   96         "ci95_low": float(lo),
   97         "ci95_high": float(hi),
   98         "p_a_better": float((diffs < 0).mean()),
   99         "separates": bool(lo > 0 or hi < 0),
  100         "n_sentences": len(references),
  101         "n_resamples": n_resamples,
  102     }
  103 
  104 
  105 def win_loss_tie(references: list[str], hyp_a: list[str], hyp_b: list[str]) -> dict:
  106     """Per-sentence outcome counts — the cheap companion to the bootstrap."""
  107     wins = losses = ties = 0
  108     for ref, a, b in zip(references, hyp_a, hyp_b):
  109         ea, na = sentence_errors(ref, a)
  110         eb, _ = sentence_errors(ref, b)
  111         if na == 0:
  112             continue
  113         if ea < eb:
  114             wins += 1
  115         elif ea > eb:
  116             losses += 1
  117         else:
  118             ties += 1
  119     return {"a_wins": wins, "b_wins": losses, "ties": ties}

benchmarks/tts-vi/tts_vi_bench/asr_judges.py
    1 """The two ASR judges for the round-trip, each configured exactly as in benchmarks/stt.
    2 
    3 **PhoWhisper-small scores. Zipformer checks. Neither is combined with the other.**
    4 
    5 The ZeroTTS vendor transcribes with two ASRs and takes the per-utterance minimum.
    6 That is not copied here, for three reasons:
    7 
    8 `min` is not a neutral operator on a *comparison*. It lowers both systems'
    9 absolute WER, which is what a vendor wants for a headline, but it moves the
   10 *delta between the two TTS systems* in a direction that cannot be stated in
   11 advance: it rewards a system whose errors are idiosyncratic to one judge and
   12 punishes one whose errors are acoustic and therefore shared by both. An operator
   13 with an unpredictable sign on the quantity that IS the deliverable is the wrong
   14 operator.
   15 
   16 Our two judges are far less symmetric than the vendor's. They used
   17 whisper-large-v3 with PhoWhisper-large — two large encoder-decoders of
   18 comparable strength, where min approximates best-of-two-equals. Ours are
   19 PhoWhisper-small (AED, INT8) and Zipformer-30M (RNN-T, INT8). A min across those
   20 mostly reproduces whichever is stronger on that acoustic condition, plus noise.
   21 
   22 And min would discard the control: `docs/development-journey.md` records a
   23 human-speech floor for each judge separately over these exact 50 VIVOS
   24 utterances. There is no recorded floor for a min-of-two column.
   25 
   26 What the second judge genuinely buys is a rank-agreement check: if both judges
   27 order the arms the same way, the conclusion is robust to the judge; if they
   28 disagree, that blocks the verdict rather than being averaged away.
   29 
   30 Both judges read their weights from `benchmarks/stt/models/` rather than keeping
   31 a copy, deliberately: the recorded human-speech floors only apply if the weights
   32 are byte-identical to the ones that produced them, and a second download could
   33 drift silently. That harness stores both as plain local directories, so neither
   34 judge resolves a repo id at run time and `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1` costs nothing.
   35 """
   36 
   37 from dataclasses import dataclass
   38 from pathlib import Path
   39 
   40 import soundfile as sf
   41 
   42 from .measure import bench_threads
   43 
   44 BENCH_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
   45 STT_BENCH = BENCH_ROOT.parent / "stt"
   46 ZIPFORMER_DIR = STT_BENCH / "models" / "zipformer-vi-30m"
   47 PHOWHISPER_DIR = STT_BENCH / "models" / "phowhisper-small-ct2"
   48 
   49 PHOWHISPER_REPO = "diepho/PhoWhisper-small-ct2"
   50 ZIPFORMER_REPO = "hynt/Zipformer-30M-RNNT-6000h"
   51 
   52 #: Human-speech WER over the same 50 VIVOS utterances, from
   53 #: docs/development-journey.md. Quoted in every table as the control: without it
   54 #: a reader cannot tell a bad synthetic voice from the judge's own error floor.
   55 HUMAN_SPEECH_FLOOR_WER = {"phowhisper-small": 0.0771, "zipformer-vi": 0.0538}
   56 
   57 
   58 @dataclass
   59 class Judgement:
   60     judge_id: str
   61     text: str
   62 
   63 
   64 class PhoWhisperJudge:
   65     """Scoring judge. faster-whisper INT8, greedy — benchmarks/stt's config.
   66 
   67     Greedy matches the STT harness exactly, so any difference between these
   68     numbers and that harness's comes from the audio and not from the decoder.
   69     """
   70 
   71     judge_id = "phowhisper-small"
   72 
   73     def __init__(self) -> None:
   74         self._model = None
   75 
   76     def load(self) -> None:
   77         from faster_whisper import WhisperModel
   78 
   79         if not (PHOWHISPER_DIR / "model.bin").exists():
   80             raise FileNotFoundError(
   81                 f"PhoWhisper weights missing from {PHOWHISPER_DIR}. Run "
   82                 "benchmarks/stt's scripts/download_models.py first — this harness "
   83                 "reads that copy so the recorded human-speech floor applies."
   84             )
   85         self._model = WhisperModel(
   86             str(PHOWHISPER_DIR),
   87             device="cpu",
   88             compute_type="int8",
   89             cpu_threads=bench_threads(),
   90         )
   91 
   92     def transcribe(self, wav_path: Path) -> str:
   93         segments, _info = self._model.transcribe(str(wav_path), language="vi", beam_size=1)
   94         return " ".join(segment.text.strip() for segment in segments)
   95 
   96     def decode_params(self) -> dict:
   97         return {"model": PHOWHISPER_REPO, "compute_type": "int8",
   98                 "beam_size": 1, "language": "vi", "threads": bench_threads()}
   99 
  100 
  101 class ZipformerJudge:
  102     """Rank-agreement judge — and the app's own STT.
  103 
  104     "Does the recognizer actually in the product understand this voice?" is a
  105     question the headline judge cannot answer. Its brittleness on synthetic
  106     audio (6,000h of real speech, no exposure to a vocoder's flat noise floor)
  107     is exactly why its output is reported separately and never merged into the
  108     score.
  109 
  110     Weights are CC-BY-NC-ND-4.0 — academic and measurement use only, which is
  111     what this is.
  112     """
  113 
  114     judge_id = "zipformer-vi"
  115 
  116     def __init__(self) -> None:
  117         self._recognizer = None
  118 
  119     def load(self) -> None:
  120         import sherpa_onnx
  121 
  122         missing = [p for p in ("encoder-epoch-20-avg-10.int8.onnx",
  123                                "decoder-epoch-20-avg-10.int8.onnx",
  124                                "joiner-epoch-20-avg-10.int8.onnx",
  125                                "tokens.txt")
  126                    if not (ZIPFORMER_DIR / p).exists()]
  127         if missing:
  128             raise FileNotFoundError(
  129                 f"Zipformer weights missing from {ZIPFORMER_DIR}: {missing}. "
  130                 "Run benchmarks/stt's scripts/download_models.py first — this "
  131                 "harness reads that copy so the recorded human-speech floor applies."
  132             )
  133         self._recognizer = sherpa_onnx.OfflineRecognizer.from_transducer(
  134             encoder=str(ZIPFORMER_DIR / "encoder-epoch-20-avg-10.int8.onnx"),
  135             decoder=str(ZIPFORMER_DIR / "decoder-epoch-20-avg-10.int8.onnx"),
  136             joiner=str(ZIPFORMER_DIR / "joiner-epoch-20-avg-10.int8.onnx"),
  137             tokens=str(ZIPFORMER_DIR / "tokens.txt"),
  138             num_threads=bench_threads(),
  139             # Shipping configuration: greedy, unbiased. Hotwords would measure
  140             # the biasing plumbing, not whether the voice is intelligible.
  141             decoding_method="greedy_search",
  142         )
  143 
  144     def transcribe(self, wav_path: Path) -> str:
  145         samples, sample_rate = sf.read(str(wav_path), dtype="float32")
  146         stream = self._recognizer.create_stream()
  147         stream.accept_waveform(sample_rate, samples)
  148         self._recognizer.decode_stream(stream)
  149         return stream.result.text
  150 
  151     def decode_params(self) -> dict:
  152         return {"model": ZIPFORMER_REPO, "quantization": "int8",
  153                 "decoding_method": "greedy_search", "hotwords": None,
  154                 "threads": bench_threads()}
  155 
  156 
  157 JUDGES = {cls.judge_id: cls for cls in (PhoWhisperJudge, ZipformerJudge)}
  158 #: The one whose numbers are the headline. The other is the agreement check.
  159 SCORING_JUDGE = PhoWhisperJudge.judge_id
  160 
  161 
  162 def download_judges() -> None:
  163     """Report on the borrowed weights. Neither judge downloads: both read benchmarks/stt."""
  164     for label, path in (("phowhisper", PHOWHISPER_DIR), ("zipformer", ZIPFORMER_DIR)):
  165         if path.exists():
  166             print(f"[asr] {label} read from {path}", flush=True)
  167         else:
  168             print(f"[asr] WARNING: {path} missing; run benchmarks/stt's "
  169                   "scripts/download_models.py", flush=True)
benchmarks/tts-vi/tts_vi_bench/text_normalize.py
    1 """Shared text normalization applied to references AND hypotheses before WER.
    2 
    3 VERBATIM COPY of `benchmarks/stt/stt_bench/text_normalize.py`. Do not edit it
    4 here. The human-speech control floors this harness quotes — PhoWhisper-vi 7.71%
    5 and Zipformer-vi 5.38% over the same 50 VIVOS utterances — were produced by that
    6 file. Diverging from it silently invalidates the comparison those floors exist to
    7 enable, which is the one coupling the "benchmark projects stay independent"
    8 convention does not cover. If the STT harness's copy changes, re-copy it and
    9 re-derive the floors.
   10 
   11 Every engine's output goes through the exact same pipeline so WER differences
   12 reflect the models, not formatting conventions. Vietnamese diacritics are
   13 preserved (only casing/punctuation/whitespace are normalized); numbers are left
   14 as written — a known WER caveat recorded in the results report.
   15 """
   16 
   17 import re
   18 import unicodedata
   19 
   20 # \w with re.UNICODE keeps Vietnamese letters and digits; everything else
   21 # (punctuation, symbols) becomes a space so word boundaries survive.
   22 _NON_WORD_RE = re.compile(r"[^\w\s]", re.UNICODE)
   23 _UNDERSCORE_RE = re.compile(r"_")
   24 _WHITESPACE_RE = re.compile(r"\s+")
   25 
   26 
   27 def normalize_text(text: str) -> str:
   28     """Normalize a transcript for WER comparison.
   29 
   30     Steps: Unicode NFC (composes Vietnamese diacritics consistently across
   31     engines), lowercase, strip punctuation/symbols, collapse whitespace.
   32     """
   33     text = unicodedata.normalize("NFC", text)
   34     text = text.lower()
   35     text = _NON_WORD_RE.sub(" ", text)
   36     # \w matches underscore; treat it as punctuation, not a word character.
   37     text = _UNDERSCORE_RE.sub(" ", text)
   38     text = _WHITESPACE_RE.sub(" ", text)
   39     return text.strip()
benchmarks/tts-vi/tts_vi_bench/clause_split.py
    1 """Port of the app's clause splitter, so the incumbent is timed as it ships.
    2 
    3 `apps/api/src/modules/translate/audio/clause-splitter.ts` splits translated text
    4 at clause boundaries *in front of* the engine, purely to cut time-to-first-audio.
    5 Its header records the measured effect: 0.648s -> 0.340s, 0.907s -> 0.393s,
    6 1.061s -> 0.813s.
    7 
    8 That makes it load-bearing for this benchmark. VieNeu has no streaming API, so
    9 the naive comparison is ZeroTTS's first streamed chunk against VieNeu's whole
   10 sentence — but the app never waits for a whole sentence. Timing it that way
   11 would overstate the incumbent roughly twofold and hand ZeroTTS a win it did not
   12 earn. The number the verdict has to beat is VieNeu *clause-split*, and producing
   13 it requires the same split the app performs.
   14 
   15 Ported rather than shared because the original is TypeScript inside the API and
   16 this is a standalone Python harness. `tests/test_clause_split.py` checks the
   17 cases the original's own comments call out, so a divergence surfaces as a failing
   18 test rather than as a quietly different number.
   19 """
   20 
   21 import re
   22 
   23 #: Shortest part worth its own synthesis call. Deliberately small: the original
   24 #: records that the measured win came from leading clauses as short as "Hello,"
   25 #: (6 chars) and "Xin chào," (9), so a larger floor would merge exactly the
   26 #: split that pays. It exists only to absorb sub-word fragments like the "Mr."
   27 #: in "Mr. Smith".
   28 MIN_PART_CHARS = 4
   29 
   30 #: Clause and sentence terminators, kept with the text they follow so the engine
   31 #: still sees the punctuation it takes intonation from. The trailing lookahead
   32 #: is load-bearing: requiring whitespace or end-of-text after the mark keeps
   33 #: "3.5" and the Vietnamese decimal comma in "1,5 triệu" from reading as
   34 #: boundaries.
   35 BOUNDARY = re.compile(r"[,;:.!?…]+(?=\s|$)")
   36 
   37 
   38 def split_into_clauses(text: str) -> list[str]:
   39     """Break text into clause-level synthesis units.
   40 
   41     Returns a single part when there is no usable boundary, and an empty list
   42     for blank input — matching the original's documented contract.
   43     """
   44     trimmed = text.strip()
   45     if not trimmed:
   46         return []
   47 
   48     parts: list[str] = []
   49     start = 0
   50     for match in BOUNDARY.finditer(trimmed):
   51         end = match.end()
   52         part = trimmed[start:end].strip()
   53         if part:
   54             parts.append(part)
   55         start = end
   56 
   57     tail = trimmed[start:].strip()
   58     if tail:
   59         parts.append(tail)
   60 
   61     return _absorb_fragments(parts)
   62 
   63 
   64 def _absorb_fragments(parts: list[str]) -> list[str]:
   65     """Fold parts too short to stand on their own into their neighbour."""
   66     merged: list[str] = []
   67     for part in parts:
   68         previous = merged[-1] if merged else None
   69         too_short = len(part) < MIN_PART_CHARS
   70         previous_too_short = previous is not None and len(previous) < MIN_PART_CHARS
   71         if previous is not None and (too_short or previous_too_short):
   72             merged[-1] = f"{previous} {part}"
   73         else:
   74             merged.append(part)
   75     return merged
benchmarks/tts-vi/tts_vi_bench/report.py
    1 """Render the speed report from whatever result files are on disk.
    2 
    3 Keyed on (engine, voice, sentence set) — not on engine alone, as the harness
    4 this was vendored from does, which would collapse four arms into two rows and
    5 two sentence sets into one.
    6 
    7 Both run tags are shown side by side rather than averaged, because the spread
    8 between them IS the significance guard for latency: two arms whose r1/r2 ranges
    9 overlap have not separated. (That guard does not transfer to WER, which is
   10 scored separately — synthesis is seeded and the ASR decodes greedily, so a
   11 second tag re-transcribes identical audio and would manufacture separation from
   12 nothing.)
   13 """
   14 
   15 import json
   16 from collections import defaultdict
   17 from pathlib import Path
   18 
   19 from .measure import latency_stats
   20 
   21 
   22 def load_arm(path: Path) -> dict:
   23     """Parse one arm's result file.
   24 
   25     Returns a `{"broken": ...}` marker rather than None for a file that cannot
   26     be summarised. Dropping it silently would let `render_report` produce a
   27     clean-looking report over exactly the arms that `--check-complete` would
   28     have rejected — the failure mode this harness exists to make impossible.
   29     """
   30     try:
   31         rows = [json.loads(l) for l in path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines()
   32                 if l.strip()]
   33     except (OSError, ValueError) as exc:
   34         return {"broken": f"{path.name}: unreadable ({exc})"}
   35     header = next((r for r in rows if r["type"] == "header"), None)
   36     footer = next((r for r in rows if r["type"] == "footer"), None)
   37     sentences = [r for r in rows if r["type"] == "sentence"]
   38     if header is None:
   39         return {"broken": f"{path.name}: no header row"}
   40     if not sentences:
   41         return {"broken": f"{path.name}: header but no sentences"}
   42 
   43     proc = [r["proc_s"] for r in sentences]
   44     total_audio = sum(r["audio_s"] for r in sentences)
   45     total_words = sum(r["n_words"] for r in sentences)
   46     streamed = [r for r in sentences if r.get("stream_ttfa_s") is not None]
   47 
   48     def med(vals):
   49         return sorted(vals)[len(vals) // 2] if vals else None
   50 
   51     return {
   52         "engine": header["engine"],
   53         "voice": header["voice"],
   54         "voice_slug": header["voice_slug"],
   55         "gender": header["gender"],
   56         "sentence_set": header["sentence_set"],
   57         "load_s": header["load_s"],
   58         "sample_rate": header["sample_rate"],
   59         "peak_rss_mb": footer["peak_rss_mb"] if footer else None,
   60         "complete": footer is not None and len(sentences) == header["num_sentences_expected"],
   61         "n": len(sentences),
   62         "n_expected": header["num_sentences_expected"],
   63         "latency": latency_stats(proc),
   64         "rtf_pooled": sum(proc) / total_audio if total_audio else None,
   65         "latency_s_per_word": sum(proc) / total_words if total_words else None,
   66         "audio_s_per_word": total_audio / total_words if total_words else None,
   67         "ttfa_whole_med": med([r["ttfa_whole_s"] for r in sentences]),
   68         "ttfa_clause_med": med([r["ttfa_clause_split_s"] for r in sentences]),
   69         "ttfa_stream_med": med([r["stream_ttfa_s"] for r in streamed]),
   70         "underrun_med": med([r["stream_underrun_margin_s"] for r in streamed
   71                              if r.get("stream_underrun_margin_s") is not None]),
   72         "underrun_worst": max([r["stream_underrun_margin_s"] for r in streamed
   73                                if r.get("stream_underrun_margin_s") is not None],
   74                               default=None),
   75         # What a listener actually waits for before audio can play without a
   76         # gap: first chunk, plus however far the stream then fell behind.
   77         # Reporting the first chunk alone credits an engine for sound it cannot
   78         # sustain.
   79         "effective_start_med": med([
   80             r["stream_ttfa_s"] + max(0.0, r.get("stream_underrun_margin_s") or 0.0)
   81             for r in streamed
   82         ]),
   83         "environment": header.get("environment", {}),
   84     }
   85 
   86 
   87 def collect(results_root: Path) -> tuple[dict, list[str]]:
   88     """({(engine, voice_slug, set): {tag: arm}}, [unparseable files])"""
   89     runs: dict = defaultdict(dict)
   90     broken: list[str] = []
   91     for tag_dir in sorted(p for p in results_root.iterdir() if p.is_dir()):
   92         for path in sorted(tag_dir.glob("*.jsonl")):
   93             if path.name == "intelligibility.jsonl":
   94                 continue
   95             arm = load_arm(path)
   96             if "broken" in arm:
   97                 broken.append(f"{tag_dir.name}/{arm['broken']}")
   98                 continue
   99             runs[(arm["engine"], arm["voice_slug"], arm["sentence_set"])][tag_dir.name] = arm
  100     return runs, broken
  101 
  102 
  103 def _fmt(value, spec=".3f", scale=1.0):
  104     return "—" if value is None else format(value * scale, spec)
  105 
  106 
  107 def render_report(results_root: Path) -> str:
  108     runs, broken = collect(results_root)
  109     if not runs:
  110         return "# TTS-vi speed report\n\nNo results.\n"
  111 
  112     any_arm = next(iter(next(iter(runs.values())).values()))
  113     env = any_arm["environment"]
  114     out = [
  115         "# Vietnamese TTS speed report — ZeroTTS vs VieNeu v3 Turbo",
  116         "",
  117         "Generated by `run_benchmark.py --render-only`. Numbers only; the verdict,",
  118         "the vendor-claim reconciliation and the caveats live in `report.md`.",
  119         "",
  120         "## Measurement conditions",
  121         "",
  122         f"- OS: **{env.get('os')} {env.get('os_release')}** ({env.get('machine')})",
  123         f"- Python {env.get('python')}, onnxruntime {env.get('onnxruntime')}",
  124         f"- Threads per engine: **{env.get('threads')}**, both engines, both sessions",
  125         f"- `HF_HUB_OFFLINE`: {env.get('hf_hub_offline')} — no network call inside a timed section",
  126         "",
  127         "> Every earlier measurement recorded in `docs/development-journey.md` was taken",
  128         "> on **Windows 11** on this same CPU. This harness runs on Linux, which is the",
  129         "> first thing to suspect when a figure here disagrees with one recorded there.",
  130         "",
  131     ]
  132 
  133     if broken:
  134         out += ["> **UNREADABLE RESULT FILES — this report is missing arms.**", ""]
  135         out += [f"> - {b}" for b in broken] + [""]
  136 
  137     incomplete = [k for k, tags in runs.items()
  138                   for t, a in tags.items() if not a["complete"]]
  139     if incomplete:
  140         out += ["> **INCOMPLETE RUN — these figures are not reportable.**", ""]
  141         for engine, voice, set_name in incomplete:
  142             out.append(f"> - {engine} / {voice} / {set_name}")
  143         out.append("")
  144 
  145     for set_name in sorted({k[2] for k in runs}):
  146         out += [f"## Sentence set: `{set_name}`", ""]
  147         out += [
  148             "| arm | tag | n | p50 s | p95 s | latency/word | audio/word | RTF | load s | peak MB |",
  149             "| --- | --- | -: | -: | -: | -: | -: | -: | -: | -: |",
  150         ]
  151         for key in sorted(k for k in runs if k[2] == set_name):
  152             engine, voice, _ = key
  153             for tag in sorted(runs[key]):
  154                 a = runs[key][tag]
  155                 out.append(
  156                     f"| {engine} / {voice} | {tag} | {a['n']} | "
  157                     f"{_fmt(a['latency']['p50_s'])} | {_fmt(a['latency']['p95_s'])} | "
  158                     f"{_fmt(a['latency_s_per_word'])} | {_fmt(a['audio_s_per_word'])} | "
  159                     f"{_fmt(a['rtf_pooled'])} | {_fmt(a['load_s'], '.2f')} | "
  160                     f"{_fmt(a['peak_rss_mb'], '.0f')} |"
  161                 )
  162         out.append("")
  163 
  164         out += [
  165             "### Time to first audio (median, ms)",
  166             "",
  167             "`clause-split` is what the app waits for today — it splits translated text",
  168             "in front of the engine. That column, not `whole`, is the incumbent baseline",
  169             "a streaming challenger has to beat.",
  170             "",
  171             "| arm | tag | whole | clause-split | streamed (first chunk) | underrun median | underrun worst | **gapless start** |",
  172             "| --- | --- | -: | -: | -: | -: | -: | -: |",
  173         ]
  174         for key in sorted(k for k in runs if k[2] == set_name):
  175             engine, voice, _ = key
  176             for tag in sorted(runs[key]):
  177                 a = runs[key][tag]
  178                 out.append(
  179                     f"| {engine} / {voice} | {tag} | "
  180                     f"{_fmt(a['ttfa_whole_med'], '.0f', 1000)} | "
  181                     f"{_fmt(a['ttfa_clause_med'], '.0f', 1000)} | "
  182                     f"{_fmt(a['ttfa_stream_med'], '.0f', 1000)} | "
  183                     f"{_fmt(a['underrun_med'], '+.0f', 1000)} | "
  184                     f"{_fmt(a['underrun_worst'], '+.0f', 1000)} | "
  185                     f"**{_fmt(a['effective_start_med'], '.0f', 1000)}** |"
  186                 )
  187         out += [
  188             "",
  189             "A **positive** underrun margin means the stream fell behind playback: the",
  190             "listener ran out of audio and heard a gap. A fast first chunk with a positive",
  191             "margin is not a win, and reporting the first chunk alone would hide it.",
  192             "",
  193             "**`gapless start` is the column to compare against `clause-split`.** It is the",
  194             "first chunk plus the worst the stream then fell behind — i.e. how long a",
  195             "player must buffer before it can run to the end without stalling. The",
  196             "first-chunk figure is what a vendor quotes; this is what a listener waits for.",
  197             "",
  198         ]
  199 
  200     return "\n".join(out) + "\n"
